# Editor system

`src/editor/`: editing a document on its preview page and writing the edits back into `report.html` exactly. The page's side is `page/` (the editor's modules, loaded only by the local preview; its editing tools a folder each, see "Where the editing tools live"); the server's is `source.mjs`, whose modules are in `source/` (finding blocks, `blocks.mjs`, and units, `units.mjs`; the preview's marks, `instrument.mjs`; patching, `apply.mjs`, and merging, `merge.mjs`, the source; commits, `commit.mjs`; block ids, `ids.mjs`) and `service.mjs` (the commit endpoints). What the editor knows of the kit's components is `src/editor/components/`, a folder each, shared by the page and the server (see "Components"). The overview of all systems is [internals.md](internals.md); the tests are in [testing.md](testing.md).

## In-place text editing

On entry, `editor.mjs` resolves the initial text hosts with the existing `firstByAttribute` index from `core.mjs`: one scan of `data-ge-block`, preserving the first match for each stringified block ID, instead of a full-document selector for every block. The two preparation passes, script-change checks, save and recovery rules are unchanged.

The live preview lets the reader fix wording on the page. The document's file (`doc.html`, or `report.html` in an older document) stays the only model; the page is a view, and every edit becomes "replace this block's source range with this text".

- **Editable blocks.** `findBlocks()` parses the source with parse5 (with source locations) and gives every block a *kind* and *capabilities*. Inside `main`: `flow` blocks are `h1`–`h4`, `p` and `li` (including those in detail cards and disclosure contents; a flowchart's step written as a node, its blocks alone, is none: its title is its first paragraph, `source/units.mjs` `flowNode`; an `li` holding lists after its text, `<li>text<ul>…</ul></li>`, is a block of its text only, ending before the lists without the whitespace ahead of them (`nested`), and their items are blocks of their own, while an `li` with a list amid its text stays read-only); `title` is the `h1` of the header (or `h1.doc-title`), the document's title; `code` is the text of a code block (`div.doc-code`: the one `code` element of its `pre`), plain text with its line breaks; `codeTitle` is its title (`.doc-code-title`), plain text on one line, which may be emptied (the kit then shows 代码块); `field` covers a figure card's direct `h3`/`h4`/`p`/`figcaption` children and the `span`s of `.doc-table-meta`, and what else a figure holds is walked as text of the document's own unless it is the kit's (a chart, a picture) or a script may draw in it (an `id`, a `data-*` attribute): a drawing made of elements (step cards and their arrows, boxes and their labels) has its words edited as `text` blocks; and a `p`, `h1`–`h4` or `li` inside a whole block (the document's own, or one of the kit's: a filter's items) or a card's drawing, no unit of its own, is a `text` block too, words only (`scan` turns such a `flow` block into one): typed in, formatted and linked, emptied, but never split (Enter says why), joined, taken out on its own or turned into another block (its "/" menu holds 公式, 链接 and 页面 alone), its element going with its block; `label` is `.doc-details-summary-label`; `cell` is a table `td`/`th`; `tab` is a `button.doc-sheet-tab` of a `.doc-sheet-tabs` row; `text` is any other element holding text and nothing but inline markup (a footer, a label in a document widget; one holding an inline element of the document's own with words in it is walked into instead, so a step's `<b>` title and `<span>` description are a block each), outside the kit's components (their labels are UI) and outside text that stays read-only, never a link and never an element with an `id` or `data-*` attribute (scripts rewrite such text). A block may contain only text, `strong/em/b/i/code/a/br/sup/sub`, the kit's color spans (below), and, kept as atoms (shown and written back as the file has them, the caret passing over them), inline formulas (`span.doc-math-inline`), links shown with their icon (`a.doc-link-mention`, as the kit writes them: anything else in one locks the block) and inline elements of the document's own (`page/own.mjs` `ownInline`: a span with a class, style or attribute of its own, a bare span, `cite`, `small`, `abbr`… and a format's tag with attributes and no words, a drawn arrow: a number badge in a heading, a citation in a cell; the page finds the same atoms as the scanner, `atomsIn`, and draws them as the document does, `data-ge-own`); the title, code and TeX only plain text; a `text` block that is an inline element takes no format from the editor, every element in it an atom (`inlineMode` "atoms": a word in `<i>`, a drawn swatch). A commit may write an inline element of the document's own only as the file has it (`checks.mjs` `knownIn`), so the page writes back what was there and nothing else. Form controls, buttons, `svg`, `summary` apart from its label, a code block holding other markup, a chart's own markup and elements with their own `contenteditable` are never blocks. A candidate holding anything else (comments, unknown tags, implied end tags, `on*` attributes, `javascript:`/`data:` links) stays read-only and records the reason, so nothing can be dropped silently. A cell with `data-sort-value` edits as any: a commit of its text writes its key too (`source/apply.mjs` `sortKey`), in the key's own scale (the new text's number times what the old key was to the old text's number: `3.4亿` keyed 340000000 edited to `3.6亿` gets 360000000); a text still holding no number keeps a missing value's key, and otherwise the key goes (the kit then sorts the cell by its text).
- **Capabilities.** `split` (Enter makes a new sibling: `p`, or `li` in lists; a heading splits into a `p`), `merge`, `remove`, `empty` (may be saved with no text), `plain` (no inline markup) and `links`. Flow blocks have all but `empty`/`plain`, code has `empty` and `plain`; figure fields and table-meta spans can be removed but not split; titles, disclosure titles and Sheet tabs only change their text (tabs and titles are plain text; the document's title may be emptied too, `empty`: an untitled document, "Title"); cells and `text` blocks can be emptied but not removed (a footer or widget label goes only with its block). A block is *bound* when something refers to its own element: another element by its id (a link, an ARIA reference, a dialog trigger) or the document's script by its id, a `data-*` attribute or its class (`keepReferenced()`, which also returns that check for one element). Only the scripts the page runs count (none, `module` or a JavaScript type: a data block's, a chart's `<script type="application/json">`, names nothing it selects), and a class only where the code can select an element by it (`classesSelected()`): a `.name` in a string literal (a selector, `querySelector(".grid i.lo")`, `d3.select("i.lo")`) or a quoted name given to a class API (`classList.add/remove/toggle/contains/replace`, d3's `.classed`, `getElementsByClassName`, `className` compared or set, a selector's `[class~=…]`), never a property the code reads (`row.lo`, d3's `.on(…)`) nor a quoted word elsewhere (an object's `"lo"` key), which kept blocks from the reader for nothing (an Agent simulation of 2026-10-28); a selector built from a variable is not seen. `gallery check` names each block a script keeps (`scriptKept()`, docs/render.md). A bound block keeps its element: it splits (the element keeps the first part) and takes the next block in (`split`, `merge`), but does not go, merge into the block before or lose all its text. Attributes alone bind nothing: an id nothing points at is an anchor like any other, `data-block-id` is the block's identity (see "Block ids"), and a section's own `aria-labelledby` binds no heading in it. Every block has the same menu; what differs is only what it can do. The server enforces the same rules.
- **Preview instrumentation.** `createEditService().preview()`, given the document as its pages see it (`read()`: an Agent's natural HTML the kit's in memory, see "Natural HTML"), adds `data-ge-block="<n>"` to each block's start tag, and `data-ge-lock="<reason>"` to each read-only candidate, in the served HTML only; remembers the source under its hash (the merge base); and appends a JSON map (per-block source, formula ranges, kind, capabilities, the token) plus a self-removing loader that imports `/__gallery/edit/src/editor/page/editor.mjs` (the preview serves the editor's browser modules by their path under `src/`; the preview server's pages import it as `/__gallery/edit/v/<stamp>/src/editor/page/editor.mjs`, the stamp of every module it serves, which the browser keeps them under for good: docs/render.md, "Live preview").
- **Browser side.** The page edits from the start: the editor rebuilds each block's DOM from its source (`prepareBlock`), reusing the already rendered KaTeX nodes as non-editable chips. The rebuild leaves the page looking as it was, so it runs through the kit's `DocKit.quietly` (render.md, "Runtime"): the kit's tables lay themselves out again once the page is idle, not in the frame editing starts in. A block whose text on the page differs from its source (a script filled it, such as a status line; formulas and `aria-hidden` parts are ignored) is left alone and marked read-only. A text block whose own text holds a long run (`src/shared/long-runs.mjs`: more than 20 characters with no place to break inside it, a path, a URL, a long identifier; [render.md](render.md), the design defaults) breaks anywhere as a whole while it is edited (`long-runs.mjs`: `data-ge-long-run`, `word-break: break-all` by a rule of no weight, so a block's own wins, a table's header and short fields; a nested list or a formula it holds breaks as the page's text does, an item of that list judged by its own text): the kit holds such a run in a span of its own in the page a reader sees, and no text the editor keeps equal to the file may hold one, so in that block an ordinary word may be parted at a line's end too while it is edited, the known compromise; every other block keeps its words whole. Judged as editing starts and again for each block whose text changes, whatever changed it (typing, a paste, undo, a rebuild from the file, a block made editable): a MutationObserver on `main` judges only the blocks its records stand in, each by its own text; a block of plain text (a code block's lines, a tab's name: `textFilters` false) is never marked, and an instance's text part is judged as any text block. Each block is its own `contenteditable` host, so the browser's selection never spans blocks; a selection across blocks is the page's own (see "Selecting text across blocks"). A frame shows only around what is being edited, and only with the reader's 显示编辑框 on (the workbench's 设置; off by default: `shared/preferences.mjs`) (`markFrames()` in `core.mjs` marks where it goes): a dashed line (1px, the accent at 45%, where a selected block's fill would be: `reachOf()` in `overlay-layer.mjs`) around the focused text when it is a unit of its own, drawn in the editor's layer (`frame.mjs`) over where the block shows (`extentOf()`, as a selected block's fill: a list item's frame takes in its marker, edge to edge with its fill) and cut where a container that scrolls cuts the block (`clipOf()`: a dialog's content, a Sheet's tab row; the fills are cut the same way), never as the element's own outline; any other unit that is not a box (a card, a table and its scope line, a disclosure, code, a formula, a Sheet tab, a document widget, text that stays read-only) carries `data-ge-frame`, and the text inside it (`data-ge-framed`: card titles and captions, cells, table-meta spans, disclosure titles, tab names, a widget's text, and blocks split off them) is framed through it while that text has the focus (`frameOf()`) and tinted itself on hover or focus (not a table's cell, hovered or edited: selected cells have the table tools' frame). Editable text has a text block's corners (3px) and, in a component, a light fill under the pointer unless its component says otherwise through `--ge-text-radius` and `--ge-text-tint` in its own style (cells, code blocks and their titles, disclosure titles, media captions and a card's fields (its title, its caption, a table's scope line: the `field` kind, `figure/page.mjs`) take no fill). Sections, Sheet areas and the other boxes (rows of columns and their columns, lists, quotes and plain divs) have no frame of their own. Selected blocks are filled instead (see "Selecting blocks"). A frame follows its element's corners, so a framed element with square corners of its own (a document widget, say) gets 8px ones while editing (`data-ge-round`), like the kit's components; media, lines and elements that clip their content keep theirs. A plain click on a link in the text opens it, as for a reader (`followLink`): in a new tab, in front of the page, where the browser would leave it behind with Ctrl or ⌘ held, as it does for a middle click, which the page takes over because a browser follows no link in editable text (a link to a file of the document's folder, which the rebuilt text writes `assets/…` as the file does, at the route the server hands the file out from), and a link to a dialog opens the dialog (the editor prevents the click and opens it, and `dialogs.js` leaves prevented clicks alone). The press stays the browser's: a drag in a link selects its text and the click that ends it opens nothing, nor does a Shift click (it takes the selection there, which the browser does not do on a link), the second click of a double click, or the press that closes something open (a link card being filled in, a formula's editor, a menu, a picker), where a selection or a selected block is no such thing and the click opens; a click also stops the card waiting to open, and no card comes to rest while something is open. A link's words are edited by going in with the arrow keys. What replaces selected text takes the marks of its start: the letters of an input method over text that starts in a link stay in the link, all of them, the page putting back what a browser takes for gone with its words; keys typed over it keep the first character there and go on after it, as at any link's end. A link in `main` outside editable text (a block the page does not edit, a card an Agent wrote) would take the page with it, and in a workbench the frame: a plain click on one with a web address opens a new tab instead (`followOutside`, as the click bubbles; a link in a drawn figure too, whose href may be `xlink:href`), and one with no address goes nowhere; one with `download` or a `target` of its own, an anchor and a click with a key held stay the browser's and the kit's, so a file block saves its file and a bookmark's card opens as it does for a reader. The link card (`links/`: 246px, a field with a thin frame that turns into a blue ring with the focus, then, for a link there is (a new one's field takes the card's width), a copy button and a remove button; for a link to a web page, after a line, the view it is shown in with ▾, 346px) opens below the selected text from Ctrl+K or the bar's link button, its field taking the focus while the text stays marked in the selection's color (drawn over it, `.ge-link-mark`), and below a link when the pointer rests on it for 0.6s (only a pointer that moved onto it: one that came under a still pointer, the next page shown where a link was just clicked or the page scrolled, opens nothing, for its card would stand over the lines below, in the way of the next click), the focus staying where it was. The field takes a web address (a host written alone gets `http://` as a `www.` address does: `example.com`, `localhost:3000`, `127.0.0.1`), what names a place of its own (another scheme, `mailto:`, `#x`, a path) or the words of a page's title. What is typed looks for a page (`links/pages.mjs`: the list of `GET /__gallery/workspace/outline` read once an opening, every column of the sidebar in its order, 写作偏好 and the sidebar's web links left out (a document under a link is in it), this page by the title it shows; a title's start, then a word's, then anywhere, then its characters in their order, no pinyin; six rows, each its icon, its title and the page it is under), an input method's letters as they come, with their apostrophes and spaces out; only a paste (`insertFromPaste`) looks for nothing. The rows stand under the field, in the card and as wide as it (`menu.mjs`, `inside`), which stays clear of the window's end by the most it has been high in this opening; a list that cannot be read is no rows, the field working as ever. Enter takes the row the keys moved to, else what reads as a whole address, else the first row (highlighted only where the words are no address), and with no row says 没有找到这个页面。, the card staying. A row clicked links to its page (the press on it closes nothing). A press elsewhere keeps an address and gives up any other words, a row no one chose among them; an emptied field takes the link away; Esc gives up. An address of a document is written as a file writes it, in every form now that the list is there (`/#<title>` too; on another port only if the list holds it), and the "/" menu's 链接 given a page takes its title for words. A link to a document shows the document's title, and the card keeps what it gave the field beside it (`shown`), so that a card left as it was changes nothing and a page's title that reads like an address stays no address; its copy button copies the address that opens it from anywhere (`wholeAddress`, as the clipboard writes it). What a file already holds stands until it is changed. A card opened by resting goes 0.3s after the pointer leaves the link and the card (and its views), unless its field was clicked. **A link's views** (`links/views.mjs`): 链接视图 (its address as its text), 标题视图 (the page's title as its text), 图标视图 (the site's icon and the page's title as a whole: `a.doc-link-mention`, `shared/components/mention.mjs`; an atom of the text as a formula is, written back verbatim, its icon a file of the document's folder that the preview serves and an export embeds, an empty `span.doc-link-mention-icon` drawn as a globe when there is none) and 书签视图 (a 网页书签 block in the link's place: the text before it stays in its block, the text after it goes on in a paragraph after the bookmark, a block left empty goes; lists and quotes split as for a paste: the clipboard's `blocksAt`). The card's view button lists them, the current one checked; a bookmark's block menu has 显示为 › to put its link back in the text (a paragraph in its place). The server reads the page (`POST /__gallery/edit/unfurl` with `parts`: "title" reads no images; "icon" keeps the icon alone; "all" a bookmark's), a spinner after the link meanwhile; a page that cannot be read gives its host as the title (暂不支持获取该网站的预览信息) and a globe; titles are cut at 100 characters; each switch is one undo step. A chip opens at a click as any link does, and only resting on it brings its card; an empty one asks for its address at a click; its field changed reads the page again; removed, its title stays as text. An address pasted alone (the plain text an address, HTML beside it holding nothing but that link: `bareAddress`, so a web page's link copied counts) becomes a link of it in any text block that takes links, a cell and a caption too (no 书签视图 where the block cannot split), and of the new paragraph when blocks are selected, with 选择视图 offered where the format bar stands for selected text and going with the link as the page scrolls (one rule, `placement.mjs` `besideText`, the one 粘贴为 uses), 链接视图 highlighted: ↑/↓ and Enter pick, Esc, a press elsewhere or typing on close it. The same rows offered after the "/" menu's 链接 are titled 显示为, at the link's end, as are the card's and a bookmark's. Our own address pasted (read with the list, which only an address of this computer asks for; one on another port is ours if the list holds it, else a web address like any) is a link as a file writes it, the document's title for its words, a block's too, and its 选择视图 offers 标题视图, highlighted, or 页面块: a link to a document has no 链接视图 or 书签视图, and no web page is read for it. The "/" menu's 链接 puts a gray 链接 where the link goes, tinted while the card asks for its address (as 公式 puts 新公式; nothing is committed meanwhile): Enter makes a link of the address there and offers its views, a press elsewhere makes it too; nothing given, or Esc, leaves the gray 链接 there, a link left empty (see "Empty blocks"), and a click on it asks again. What is typed after a "/" or an "@" while its menu is open gets no space beside it (`autoSpace` waits), so none is left where the menu puts a formula or link; a link shown with its icon stands a space apart from nothing (no `spaceAround`, none from the kit). A copied chip pastes as a chip, its icon kept within the document it came from (a globe elsewhere); for other programs it is a link of its title. The editor handles Enter, Backspace/Delete at block edges (merge with the neighbouring block of the same kind; in an empty block, remove it, together with a list or quote left empty, and move to the neighbouring block), arrow keys at the first/last line or edge of a block (←/→ go to the end of the text block before or the start of the one after, ↑/↓ to the last line of the one before or the first line of the one after, in the page's order (`places()` in `caret.mjs`: every text block the reader can see, the page's title, a disclosure's title and content, a code block's title and its code, which opens for editing, table cells row by row for ←/→, columns one after the other; a divider, a picture, a card or a formula block (no text to type in) passed over, and a closed disclosure's content, which the browser still lays out but never draws, too; an empty line of code is a line to stop on; in a table (the user's rule), ↑/↓ on a cell's first or last line go to the cell above or below in the column at the caret's position, by the table's slots (`vertical()`: from a merged cell the row past all its rows, among the cells under its columns the one at that position), from the first row up and the last row down to the block before or after the table, and into a table from the block before (after) it its first (last) row's cell there; a table whose cells tile no rectangle keeps the page's order), at the same horizontal position via `caretPositionFromPoint`; where the caret is goes by the character after it (a collapsed range measures the end of the line before wherever the caret stands at a line wrapped there), and where the browser wrapped a line, the end of the one and the start of the next being one place, by where the browser draws it (`atLineEnd()`: End, or a click past the line's end, puts it at the end; Home, a click at the next line's start and a range selected, at the start; the selection moved to its line's start tells, then goes back), a caret the keys put at a wrapped line's end going there as End does (`selectOn()`); a run of up/down presses keeps the column it started at, as text editors do, so a short line or block does not pull the caret left for the lines after it, inside a block too, and any other key, a click or typing ends the run; a line reached this way is scrolled into view, in an open dialog's content as well, with the room the box keeps clear at its edges, its scroll-padding where it has one (a detail card's content keeps its padding: a line of its last block takes it to its very end, as the browser's own following and `scrollIntoView` do there), else 24px (`reveal()` in `caret.mjs`, by whole pixels away from the line: a scroll by a fraction the browser rounded down, a line on the window's last line was left a fraction of a pixel past it); the text an input method puts in is followed as the browser follows a key typed (`showCaret()`, after `compositionend` and an input of text: Chrome follows the composition as it is typed but not the text put in for it, so a phrase that wrapped onto a new line stood in a detail card's padding, or below the window's edge, until the next key); and with the caret in a detail card's last block whose end is in view the content goes on to its very end (`showEnd()`, after a key, after text put in and where the caret is put in a block: the scroll-padding keeps room under the caret's line, which a component holds room of its own under, a code block's, a highlight block's or a callout's, a table's last row, so the content stopped a few pixels short of its end; the caret's line stays in view, and a last block taller than the card whose end is out of view moves nothing), paste (the clipboard's `text/html` through `pastedLines()` in `core.mjs`: the format bar's marks (bold, italic, inline code, links where the block takes them, the kit's ink classes, super- and subscript, a bold or italic `style`) are rebuilt as kit markup, a formula chip of the page is kept and other KaTeX becomes its TeX; any other markup gives only its text, and blocks and line breaks start new lines; plain text where there is no HTML, in code, and where the block takes no marks; several lines become several blocks where the block may split: `clipboard/`'s paste, see "Pasting"), inline formatting, links (Ctrl+K), and undo/redo. Text steps are snapshots of a block's own content (`ownHTML()`/`setOwnHTML()`: `innerHTML`, but a list item's nested lists are never part of it, nor of its serialization, caret positions (`textEnd()`), character offsets or line boxes); structural steps (split, merge, remove, multi-line paste) record the child lists of the containers involved, so undo puts the same nodes back (moving only the nodes that differ: a node already where it belongs is never taken out, so the caret in it and the page's scroll stay; taking all of the page's blocks out and back in once scrolled the page to its top), and a text block among those containers (a list item whose lists change) has its own content recorded too. A caret put after an item's lists (a click below them) goes to the end of its text. Typing Chinese next to a Latin letter or digit, or the other way round, adds a space between them where there is none (`autoSpace()`: only around the text just typed or composed, never in code, formulas or a tab's name). A number and the magnitude or measure word after it stay together (`3.6亿`, `5个`, `2024年`, `30天`, `第3名`, `3万5千`: `numberWithUnit` in `shared/inline-spacing.mjs`), and a block whose own text writes Chinese and Latin or digits together more often than apart is typed in its way, no space added (`writtenTogether`). An edit that leaves such a space without its reason takes it out in the same step (`typing/spaces.mjs`; the user's of 2026-11-19: letters typed between Chinese characters by mistake and deleted one by one left both spaces, and Chinese typed after a word, in front of the space after it, left that space between two Chinese characters; narrowed by the review of 2026-11-20, where Latin punctuation typed took the space between English words). A space that stood alone between such a pair (or between inline code or a formula and a word, or two of them) before the edit, right beside what the edit changed, loses its reason in two ways only: the edit put Chinese or a Chinese mark beside it (a "、", "／" or "＠" that opens the "/" or "@" menu is looked at once the menu is closed, the slash menu's and the "@" menu's `over`: still there, as any mark; taken into a block with the menu's query, nothing), or it deleted the Latin letter, digit, inline code or formula beside it; and it goes only where no such pair stands either side of it now and what is on its other side is no Latin word, code or formula, which keeps it whatever is typed or deleted beside it ("Run `npm`, install", "Use the manager (`npm`)", "甲 `b` ，乙"). Latin punctuation and symbols typed never take one ("." typed after a word by mistake and deleted again leaves the line as it was), and "+", "#" or "%" after a letter or digit end a word as the rule reads a space beside it, as pangu.js has it ("C++ 语言" keeps its space), though `autoSpace` puts none after one, as before (0.3.0's "100+种", "3#楼", "30%的", none of them a sign of a block written together either: `writesTogether`). A line break put beside it (Shift+Enter) takes it whatever is on its other side, the reader's own too, as Enter does: a line's edge is no neighbour. Two spaces where one stood go to one: a space typed beside the rule's one (the caret after it; the space is none of the reader's), or a deletion bringing two of the rule's together (the Chinese or the code between two words deleted). It reads the text, not who wrote the space, so the editor's, an Agent's and a pasted one go alike, after an undo or a reload too (`lineText`: a non-breaking space a space, the pads left out, inline code and a formula one item each, a break or a held block the line's edge, an empty chip nothing). The text as the edit begins is taken at its beforeinput (a composition's at compositionstart, so nothing goes while it composes; a cut's and a paste's at their events: execCommand's input has no beforeinput), used once (every `after()` lets it go, matched or not; an edit no step of the rule's follows, a menu's query, forgets it; an input's is gone after its task) and compared with the text once it is done (`editOf`: the edit lies between the selection before it and the caret after it, which settles it within a run of spaces), after `autoSpace` and before the step is recorded (onInput, onCompositionEnd, the paste's `insertNodes`), so one undo brings back the letters with both spaces. A deletion never takes a space the reader typed with the space bar, theirs to delete, and typing takes one only for Chinese put beside it (remembered while the block's text runs on from their typing in this page, forgotten at any other change of it). Enter takes the rule's space right at the break, whatever is on its other side (so no line starts or ends with one), in its step (`split`, before the block splits), never one with the caret in code, a formula or a chip, which the break goes through; a space with no pair beside it (one the reader typed at a line's end) is none of the rule's. A space deleted itself is not put back. Markers are `data-ge-*` attributes rather than JavaScript references, so a restored snapshot keeps them. IME composition is left to the browser: key handling skips `isComposing`/`keyCode 229`, and the change is recorded on `compositionend`. The browser's own formatting commands are blocked; formatting goes through `marks.mjs` (below). Drop-moves are blocked. Clicks inside a disclosure title do not toggle the disclosure; the pointer over it is the text cursor, as over a code block's title (`components/details/page.mjs`), its arrow keeping the kit's pointer. The room around its words (5px above and below them, `--doc-block-title-padding`, and the row's right end) is the title's own, the label's padding (`styles/details.css`, on the text one edits), so a press there is in the title too and only the arrow's column opens and closes the block. A Sheet tab must keep switching Sheets on a click, so `prepareBlock` moves its name into a span of its own (the block), which is `contenteditable` only while the tab is renamed: 重命名 in the tab's menu, or F2 while it has focus, selects the whole name. The span gets `user-select: text` meanwhile (Chromium otherwise gives clicks inside a button to the button). While renamed, the tab is drawn as an editing pill, arrow keys stay in the name, and neither clicks nor keys reach `sheets.js` (the space bar would click the tab, arrows would switch Sheets). Enter or leaving the name keeps it with its spaces tidied; Esc, or an emptied name, puts the name from before back, recorded like typing so it undoes with it; switching to another window does not end renaming. Undo and redo also work with focus on a tab or on the page. While a modal dialog is open, the editor's panels and notice move into it (otherwise they would be inert). Hovering read-only text shows the reason after a short delay; clicking it says the reason. There is no toolbar: what the editor tells the reader is a notice at the bottom of the window (`notices.mjs`), the workbench's own toast when the page is in one (the session's `notice`) and one that looks the same when it is not; a note shows for 3 seconds (a click into editable text takes it away), an error for 6 (and until the next edit), a prompt for 15 (or until it is replaced). Undo and redo are Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Y, exporting is the workbench's.
- **Page blocks.** A page block (页面块) is 图标视图's element with a document's address, not a kind of atom of its own: the address says what it is. The file holds `<a class="doc-link-mention" href="../<slug>/doc.html"><span class="doc-link-mention-icon"></span>标题</a>` (`#<block id>` after it for a block; within a page `#<block id>`), written back verbatim as any atom, its title a snapshot from when it was made: an Agent reads plain words, and a rename writes no document that only links to the one renamed. What the page shows is drawn by the server (`render/page-chips.mjs`, [render.md](render.md) "Page blocks"), so it reaches the page as a chip whose words differ from its source; `matchesSource` compares no atom, `prepareBlock` keeps the drawn chip and takes the source from the file, so showing a new title commits nothing and changes no hash, and undo restores the chip by its key. 批注 read a chip by its source's words on the page (`annotate/anchors.mjs`, `sourceWords`), which is what the server reads. Icon and title with the thin gray line under the title alone (an atomic icon takes no underline), the size of the text; a gray fill under the pointer for a reader as for the editor (`mention.css`, by the address: a web chip has none), a click opens the document, Backspace deletes it whole, the caret never goes in, and resting opens no card: to lead elsewhere it is deleted and made again. The kit draws a page for an empty icon of an address that is no web page's. Its document gone, the server marks it (`data-doc-gone`): its words, gray, no line, and a click says which, as the workbench and a presentation do: 这个页面已删除。 for one in the trash, 没有找到这个页面。 for any other. The "/" menu's 页面 (`inline:page`) puts a gray 页面 where it goes with the link card, in its page form: 输入页面标题, the first pages listed at once. Enter takes the page an address names; another address makes nothing, said with the card staying; else the row highlighted; with no row the words make a **new subpage**: `POST /__gallery/workspace/create` with this page for `parent` and the description and language the workbench's 新建 gives, the card holding the words until the answer, then the block; the page does not reload or move, the caret stays, and `workspace` (session.mjs) has the sidebar read the list again and open this page's row. Undo takes the block away, not the page. A failure says so, the gray block and the card staying; `?solo` (写作偏好, no place in the tree) makes none. With nothing typed Enter is Esc. Esc or a press elsewhere (which keeps only an address of a page) leaves the gray 页面, in the file `<a class="doc-link-mention" data-doc-page></a>`, a page block on reload, and a click asks again. Pasted into another document a chip stays a chip, its address good there (`#<block id>` becomes its page's). What the clipboard gives another program says the title the page shows (`core.mjs` `shownWords`), and a chip pasted is shown as the server draws one once the list of pages is read (`links/` `drawPasted`), its source what the copy said. Scenarios `pageChip`, `pageChipReload`, `pageChipShown`, `pageChipCreate`, `pageChipCopied`, `pageChipColumn`.
- **"@".** `at-menu/`, beside the "/" menu and wired where it is. "@" or "＠" typed at a block's start, after white space, `( ) [ ]`, a non-ASCII character or a whole in the text, where the text takes links and not in inline code or a link, opens it; a cell's text does, a caption's too, a title and code do not, and `a@b` does not. Only one typed does (the `data` of the input or of a composition's end): a plain "@" stays plain whatever is deleted after it. It is drawn, not typed: a pale field (`--ge-field`, 0.5px corners, no outline round it: see the "/" menu's) with 链接到页面或块… over what is typed (`.ge-at`, mounted over the page as the card's marks are, a follower of `frames.mjs`, as a table laid out again in a frame of its own moves the "@"), the hint gone with the first letter, an input method's too, where words follow the caret and where it would pass the block's edge. The block holds the characters typed and nothing else, so `ownHTML`, offsets, undo and a composition know nothing of it. Under it the pages (`links/pages.mjs`), at once and as they are found, an address's page first (`placeIn`). Enter or Tab with words typed take the first row, ↑/↓ another, a click any: a page block in place of the "@…", all of it wherever the caret is in it, one undo step back to it. With nothing typed or no row Enter is a plain "@" and an ordinary Enter, whatever row the pointer rests on; with nothing typed only the keys choose one. It has its Enter before a component that takes Enter from the capture phase (a cell's, a caption's: `typedKey`). A press elsewhere, Esc, the caret leaving what is typed, a space right after the "@" and the window losing the focus (the workbench takes it, and no press reaches the page) leave a plain "@". It gives up by one formula: committed characters less those when something was last found come to 2. So a first character finding nothing goes on, a second gives up, a slip after a hit is not lost, and one commit of 周报 finding nothing gives up at once; letters being composed filter, their apostrophes and spaces out, and do not count; what may become an address (a start of `http://`, `https://`, `127.0.0.1`, `localhost`, or `#`) is no miss. A paste goes three ways: a page's or block's address is a page block, a web address takes the "@…"'s place by the ordinary paste with its views, anything else goes in after it as text. "@" makes no page. While it stands, and while the "/" menu does, nothing is committed (`blocked` in `editor.mjs`, which holds a reload too), so the file never holds half of what was typed for a menu; `closeTyped` lets go on the window's blur, on Ctrl+S and before the page is left, so that a menu forgotten never holds a save. The crash buffer can bring back plain words, what a press elsewhere leaves. Scenarios `mentionMenuIme`, `mentionNeverCommitted`, `slashNeverCommitted`, `mentionGiveUp`, `mentionPlaces`, `mentionLook`, `mentionStaysPlain`, `mentionTakesAll`, `mentionInCell`, `pageRowsLong`.
- **Inline formatting.** Selecting text inside one block (not the title) shows a floating card once the mouse is released, its corner 4px right of and 8px below the selection's last character, also when the selection runs over several lines (8px above the selection when there is no room below), fading in over 0.12s (no fade out); its sizes, colors, shadow and buttons (as wide as four buttons, 152px, 6px padding, 14px radius and a bar's lighter shadow (`--ge-shadow-bar`, the finish of 2026-11-17: 8px padding and the card's shadow until then), 32×28px buttons 4px apart and 8px round with the editor's own 20px line icons, every line of them 1.25px (bold's B heavier), the active ones a blue glyph with no tint behind it; its panels (the colors, the line, 更多, the types beside it) are cards that grow out of their button's corner and fade out in 90ms; 斜体 and 删除线 are the owner's picks of 2026-11-17; 应用格式 is the clear-format T with a check where that one has its cross), with its rows of buttons on top and its type row at the bottom. Four buttons to a row. The top row: the format brush (应用格式; Ctrl+⇧+H does what its click does, below), the color "A" (below), bold (Ctrl+B) and underline (below; Ctrl+U puts the plain underline on, red and 2px, or takes one off). With nothing selected the format keys (Ctrl+B, Ctrl+I, Ctrl+U, Ctrl+E, Ctrl+⇧+X) keep the format for the next text typed at the caret (a pending format: the status line says so; the format applies to what is typed, also what an input method commits, and typing on stays in it; pressed again it is let go; the caret moving elsewhere forgets it; inside bold text the key takes the bold off the next text). The second: clearing the format (Ctrl+Alt+\; Ctrl+\ alone stays the workbench's sidebar: bold, italic, underline, strikethrough, code and both colors; links stay), inline code (Ctrl+E), a formula (Ctrl+⇧+E, see below) and "更多" (three dots, blue while the selection has what it holds), which opens below it, resting on it or with a click, a short list of what is used less: a link (Ctrl+K), strikethrough (Ctrl+⇧+X or Ctrl+⇧+S), italic (Ctrl+I), a detail card (详情卡片: a card above the words, which become its entry, a chip with a rounded fill and a small open icon; see "New blocks and conversions") and, where the selection can be annotated, 批注 (Ctrl+Alt+M; "Annotations" below). On the page an entry opens as a link does: a click on its words or its icon opens the detail card, the arrow keys go into its words, and resting on it shows its own card instead of the link card: the detail card's name, 打开, and 移除 (the words stay as text; the detail card is no longer saved) (`links/`), each with its icon, name and shortcut. Below a line, the block's type (its icon and name; resting on it opens them, a click too) opens the types it can be turned into as a grid of icons (`type-grid.mjs`, the current one marked; one undo step through `convert.mjs`); the row shows only for a block that can be turned into something; over text across blocks it is their types together (below, "Selecting text across blocks"). Every button names itself in a dark tooltip (`tooltip.mjs`), nothing more than its name (应用格式, 文字颜色, 加粗, 下划线, 清除格式, 代码, 公式, 更多) and its shortcut on a second line (`shortcut()`: Ctrl+⇧+S, ⌘⇧S on a Mac); the brush's says what it remembers above its shortcut; each type's tooltip gives its Markdown shortcut. The format brush (`format-bar/brush.mjs`, the reader's idea) remembers the formats last set on a selection: bold, italic, strikethrough, a text color, a background and an underline with its color and weight (not inline code, formulas or links). Formats set on one selection add up (bold, then red: both); the first one set on another selection starts it over; taking a format off the selection it remembers takes it out of the brush, while taking formats off other text leaves the brush alone; text it was just put on counts as its selection, so a format set there adds to it. A click puts what it remembers on the selected text as one undo step (bold joins bold next to it) and says so; with nothing remembered yet it says how it works. It is remembered in `localStorage` (`storage.mjs`, which the colors, formulas and blocks used lately share: `gallery-edit:format-brush`), so it survives the reload after a save. The "A" shows the selection's text color and background (its letter in the color, its square filled with the background; the defaults when it has none or they differ). A click on it opens the colors below it, its left edge with the "A"'s (above it when there is no room below): 190px, three groups, 最近使用 (the last five colors picked, the last first, remembered as `gallery-edit:recent-colors`), 文本颜色 and 背景颜色 (默认 and the kit's nine colors each), five 26px swatches to a line, each ringed in its own color, the selection's own colors ringed strongly, each color's name ("红色文本", "黄色背景") in its tooltip. A pick colors the selected text; the panel stays open for the other color. The underline button's line shows the selection's underline, its color and weight (the icon's own line when the selection has none; the button never turns blue). A click on it opens the line below it: 线的颜色, none ("无下划线", taking an underline off), the red (the default), orange, blue, purple and the text's own color ("文字色"), each a U underlined so, and below them 线的粗细, thin (1px), medium (2px, the default) and thick (3px), drawn plain, the selection's ringed. A pick puts that facet on the selected text as one undo step, keeping the other it had (red and 2px where it had no underline) (`underlineColor` and `underlineWeight` in `marks.mjs`: facets of the `u` element itself, a class each, `doc-underline-…`, split at the selection's edges like any mark; taking the underline off takes the styled ones off too). A second click on the "A" or on underline closes its panel, as do Esc, a press elsewhere and the selection moving on to other text; the panels fade in and out in 0.12s, move left when they would leave the window on the right, and stand above their button when there is no room below. The formula button (`formula/`, disabled in inline code; over a formula it edits that one) turns the selected text into a formula at once: its TeX is the text without the `$…$` or `\(…\)` around it, and the formula box opens below the formula (with no button: 400px, the TeX in a monospace field, KaTeX's reason below when it cannot read it, no preview: the formula in the text follows what is typed, tinted while it is edited, a gray "√x 新公式" when empty, a gray "√x 无效的公式" when it does not read, both tinted while their box is open: the chip it stays once the box is left, nothing moving as it closes). A formula's fill, under the pointer and while its box is open, is one rectangle around all of it, however tall, and moves nothing: its `.katex` with the padding its drawing reaches past that inline box by, measured for the formula that shows a fill and written in a style sheet of the page's own (`chip-fills.mjs`), so that no attribute enters a block's snapshots; the box stands clear of all of it (`chipBox`). A click on any formula in the text opens the box for it again (a formula of the file keeps its own tags, `formulaParts()`; one not written as `\(…\)` in a span is left alone). However the box is left, what is in it is kept as one undo step (Ctrl+Enter, Esc once no group's symbols are open, a press elsewhere, in the workbench around the page too, Ctrl+S, the page reloaded or left for another document, Enter when all of the field is selected, as it opens on a formula or after Ctrl+A, a formula pasted there kept at once; else Enter is a new line, for a formula of several lines), the caret after it (one emptied stays, empty, as the "/" menu's 公式 given nothing does): a chip like the document's own formulas (`newFormula()` in `core.mjs`), saved as the kit's `<span class="doc-math-inline">\(…\)</span>`. TeX that does not read is kept too, it may be a stage on the way: the file has it as typed (a `\)` in it too; a reader sees the same gray chip, the kit's (render.md, the kit's `math.js`), also where KaTeX found no end to it), the page a gray "√x 无效的公式" chip (`data-ge-empty="invalid"`, marked by `prepareBlock` when the page opens), which a click opens with its words; `gallery check` and an export take it, Markdown has its TeX. Nothing commits while the box is open (the autosave waits, as for an input method). A document without formulas is served without KaTeX, so the box loads the dev server's copy the first time; once saved, the formula brings the kit's math back to the page. Esc closes an open panel first, then the card. The colors are nine, for text and for backgrounds, plus the defaults: the kit's classes `doc-text-{gray,brown,orange,yellow,green,blue,purple,pink,red}` and `doc-highlight-{…the same nine}` (defined in the kit's `styles/base.css` with `light-dark()`), never inline styles, so they follow the theme and survive export. A background on words takes the class's words' fill (`--doc-mark`), a step deeper than the same class's fill on a block, and square (the user's of 2026-11-06), so the format bar's 背景颜色, 最近使用 and its "A" show that one, and the block menu's 颜色 › and a table cell's bar the block's fill (they draw their swatches as `--doc-fill`: `block-menu/colors.mjs`, `table/cell-bar.mjs`) (`inlineFillLook`). `formatRange()` inserts two comment markers at the selection, splits any element of the same family that straddles a marker (its copies keep the author's attributes), strips that family inside the selection, wraps the selection in the new element, then unwraps elements left empty, merges identical neighbours and restores the selection by character offsets (a formula chip counts as one). Only the selected part changes; bold, italic, underline (`u`), strikethrough (`s`) and code toggle off when the whole selection already has them; a new color replaces the old one, and "default" or "none" removes it. Inline code refuses a selection holding a formula (math is not rendered inside `code`). Text made inline code (here or by its Markdown shortcut) and a formula made or changed in the formula box stand an ordinary space apart from a word right beside them (a letter, a digit or a Chinese character): a space goes into the text where there is none (`spaceAround` in `src/shared/inline-spacing.mjs`, which the kit's reading view uses too: it looks out of a wrapper such as bold or a link to the word beside it, and stops at the edge of the line), none beside a space, punctuation or the line's edge, as typing beside them gives (`typing/`); pasted text is spaced the same way, in itself and where it joins the text around it; the kit gives inline code no margin of its own (styles/base.css), so the text's own spaces part it from the words, never twice. The document's own text gets the same where it has none: the kit puts the space in as it renders (`runtime/spacing.js`, docs/render.md), and `prepareBlock` does it again in the text it rebuilds for editing, the baseline the commits compare with taking it while the source they send as the block's does not, so a block takes it into the file only with the reader's edit of it (`matchesSource` ignores whitespace for this). Two pieces of inline code with nothing between them any more (what stood between deleted, two blocks joined) are one (`joinCode` in `marks.mjs`), the caret at the join inside it. A formatting step is one undo step and brings its selection back on undo and redo.
- **Serialization.** Text is escaped; spaces are written as the page shows them: the non-breaking spaces the browser types to keep a space visible while editing (at the end of a text, before a mark, next to another space), with the spaces beside them, become one ordinary space (`spaced()` in `core.mjs`, on `escapeTyped` of `src/shared/escape.mjs`), so they never glue words together in the file and lines never break early because of them; runs of ordinary spaces (the source's indentation) stay as they are; source inline elements keep their tag and attributes; elements the browser created are normalized (`<b>` → `<strong>`, links keep only `href`, and the kit's `doc-dialog-trigger` class on a link that opens a detail card); a `span` keeps only the kit's color classes, and other unknown wrappers are unwrapped; formula chips emit their exact source; an element of the editor's own in a text block (`data-doc-dev`: the words an Agent took out, shown where they were, "Agent's changes") is none of it, the text on either side of it written as one text (`serializeNodes`). The server accepts a `span` only when its sole attribute is a `class` made of those color classes. A block whose serialization equals its baseline produces no edit.
- **What changed.** `collectEdits()` finds the page's changes against the layout and baselines the last commit left: `[{id, parts}]` for text blocks (`{keep: true, html}` reuses the original tags, `{tag, html}` is a new sibling, an empty list removes the block) and the changes of units (below). A new paragraph or list item split off an original text block goes with it, as a part, when only such blocks lie between them (`partOwner()`, previous first, then next); every other new block in a box is a block of its own (see "New blocks and conversions"), and one outside every box with no such neighbour is unplaced. A block that must keep text but was emptied (a new section's title among them) is blank. A free text block (a paragraph, 小字, a heading, a list item; caps `empty`) may be empty: emptied, or made and left empty, it is written as an empty element, which the kit shows one line high (`flow.css`), and Backspace in it deletes it; a list item holding blocks is written with no text over them too, its title line kept however empty, and the page gives it a line of its own, a line break ahead of them (`keepLine()`, by the kit's rule and with the kit's line break, `br.doc-item-line`, which an export of the view takes off: `keepsLine` of `src/shared/components/list.mjs`, an item of a list whose markers the kit draws in a flow, an empty bold or link before them no text, never an item of a list with a class of its own or of one in a whole block, a card or a cell, whose look is the document's, a widget's grid laid out by its children; the page opened on such an item, a paste at its start and whatever is typed or deleted in it give it one, as Backspace leaves one, and so does the list's tidier for an item a structural step leaves so, one the file writes empty that Tab gave the next item, items pasted or dropped with no text over what they hold: `tidy` in `components/list/page.mjs`, scenario `emptyItemMade`), so that a click on its marker or an arrow key puts the caret there, and a caret put at the end of its text stands before that line break, past which there is no line of its (`placeCaret()`: undo once put it after it, and what was typed went into the paragraph the item held); a step that puts the caret back where it was (Tab, Shift+Tab, Enter or Backspace taking a nested item out) counts the item's own text alone, never the words of a block it holds (`ownEnd` in `marks.mjs`), so that on such an item the caret stands on its line again; the lines ArrowUp and ArrowDown enter an item by are its own text's, none of what a block it holds shows (`lineRects` in `caret.mjs`: a task board's labels are none); and its handle stands by that line, where its marker is (`lineOffset` in `handle/`), not by the first line of what it holds, whose handle would take its place as the pointer reached it (once such an item was blank until it had words again: the file kept its text, and the words a paste at its start took after it were in the file twice) (an empty paragraph once went with its commit, so a highlight block whose only paragraph was empty came back with nothing to write into). Neither is committed until the reader fixes it: the page flags an unplaced block and says why. It reads the page in one pass for what it looks up by id (`firstByAttribute()`: each value's first element in document order, as `querySelector` would find it, the blocks still on the page, the boxes, the units converted or put back): attribute selectors are not indexed, and a search per block made every collect O(blocks × elements), 35-90 ms on a large document at every pause in typing and many times over whenever the page was left. The commits' `collect()` (its marks written first) gives the answer it gave while neither the page nor what the commits know of it changed since (`generation()`: any mutation of the document but `<html>`'s own attributes, through a `MutationObserver` whose records are taken when asked, and an answer taken in, a decision on a conflict, a block put back, a unit to name); its callers hand one answer on within a task (the count, the commit built and the crash buffer from one collect). The commit endpoint (`POST /__gallery/edit/commit`, see "Commits") requires the exact `Host` (`127.0.0.1:<port>` or `localhost:<port>`), a matching `Origin`, the `X-Gallery-Token` header, a JSON body under 2 MiB and a kebab-case slug, and only writes `<workspace>/<slug>/doc.html` (and, for a title edit, `report.json`). Every part is re-parsed in the context it will be written to (cells inside a table), so it cannot close its element. Requests run one at a time.
- **Structure in a block's words.** A text block's words are text and inline markup (and a table cell's own lists): block structure in them (a list, an item, a paragraph, a division…: `STRUCTURE` in `core.mjs`) is none of the page's steps' making, and serialization writes none of it: a list there is dropped, and a block after an item's text that is no unit and none made on the page (a division the browser's own Enter puts there) is no part of its text, so such a block wrote its words away. The user met it in a highlight block (2026-11-12): Enter in the middle of a second-level item split a block that was not the item, its tail a new paragraph holding copies of the item and of its list (`p > li > ul > li`: two black markers on one line, the paragraph's 输入 / 选择类型 over them, and the paragraph written empty while the page still showed its words); the item had no `data-ge-block`, the block around it its marks. No step found here leads there (Enter in every container, built from the file, by typing, "/", Tab, paste, copy, drag, undo and redo, with the Agent's changes shown, an input method's Enter): the browser's own editing does what is closest to it, in a text block left without its marks (an item with no `data-ge-block` that stays `contenteditable`: `project()` of `sync-state.mjs` takes the mark off an element whose block the commits' table names no more and leaves it editable; which step leaves the table so was not found), where hostOf finds no block and Chrome's Enter puts the tail in a division the item's text leaves out. So three rules close it, whatever leads there. Enter splits only with the caret in the block's own words (`splitsAt()`: before the blocks an item holds, in no structure inside them), else it says why (`STRAY_SPLIT` of `typing/`) and does nothing; a text block left without its marks (`contenteditable` and its `data-ge-kind`, no `data-ge-block` or `data-ge-new`) takes no browser Enter either (`unmarkedText` in `editor.mjs`, on keydown and on an input method's `insertParagraph`). And `collectEdits()` finds every text block holding such structure, changed or not (`strayHosts()`: the text block each element of it stands in, `strayIn()` asked once a block), and returns them as `strays`, held back in `blank` (never written, never taken out as emptied, nor the block one split off it is written with); while one stands the commits write nothing at all (`build`; the crash buffer apart), as what the page lost track of may be anywhere (a list's words taken into a paragraph, the list gone from its place), the save state is `held`, the block is flagged (`data-ge-flag="check"`) and the reader is told to undo (`checkChanges`), which writes everything again. Scenario `nestedEnterStray`; `tests/unit/editor.mjs` holds the rules.
- **Deleting blocks.** Besides text blocks, `findUnits()` marks *units*, the blocks the reader can select and delete, and the *boxes* that hold them: `main`, the document's header (the lines under the title: paragraphs, small text among them; the title itself is no unit, it is the document's name), Sheet panels, sections, highlight panels (the opening summary is one), callouts, plain divs, rows of columns and their columns, lists, quotes, disclosure and dialog contents. A unit is an element child of a box: a paragraph (a line of the header among them), heading, list or item, quote, code block, formula, callout, card, row of columns (and a column in it), table, disclosure, section or custom block. A Sheet's tab row is a box too: each `button.doc-sheet-tab` is a unit (kind `tab`) that takes its panel (the shell's `[data-doc-sheet]` child with the same key, elsewhere in the file) along; the panels are boxes of their own. Not units: the document header itself and its title (the document's name), dialogs (their content is a box), other buttons and form controls, scripts, elements with their own `contenteditable`, and elements with implied end tags. Only the kit's containers hold units of their own: the header, sections (a form control or other non-block element in one just stays put; words straight in one make it a single unit, which is why the Agent's loose words are made paragraphs before a page sees them: "Natural HTML"), highlight panels (the summary among them), rows of columns (`.doc-columns`) and their columns (`.doc-column`), disclosure and dialog contents, Sheet panels and tab rows, and class-less lists, quotes and plain `<div>`s, a plain div only directly inside a section, a highlight panel, a disclosure, a dialog or a column (the same flows the kit's `styles/flow.css` styles, see "Flows" under Design defaults). Anything with a class of its own is a component, a document widget say: one unit that moves and goes as a whole, which nothing is put into or taken out of (its text stays editable, and scripts keep it from being deleted when they use it); so is a div holding inline content or form controls. A unit's range takes along the comments on the lines right above it (no blank line between) and, for a `.doc-table-wrap`, the `.doc-table-meta` right above it (its *lead*). Every list item is a box too (kind `nest`, numbered after all the others so theirs stay as they were), whose units are the lists it holds after its text; one without a list may be given one (Tab, a drop under it). A box is *pure* when it holds nothing but units, leads and their comments. A unit whose id, `data-*` attribute name or custom class appears in the document's inline script outside it (`#id`, a quoted id or class, `.class`, the attribute name or its `dataset` form), or whose id another element outside it refers to (`href="#id"`, a drawing's `url(#id)` in any attribute, its `style` too, `data-doc-dialog-open`, `aria-*` id references, `for`...), is *kept*: a card whose id the script names (`DocCharts.get("#id")`), a figure whose marker or gradient another figure draws with (one naming only its own keeps nothing), the Sheet shell, linked tables and state widgets. A tab is also kept when a script that reads the Sheet state (`DocSheets`, the state `"sheets-<name>"` or its `"sheet"`, `data-active-sheet`) names its key where a tab stands (`sheetKeyRead()` in `units.mjs`: the tab a state starts on or is given, `sheet: "key"`, `.set("key")`, `.set("sheet", "key")`; one compared with the tab shown, `=== "key"`, `case "key":`; the area's mark, `[data-active-sheet="key"]`), never wherever the key's word stands in the document's code (a D3 drawing's `.attr("text-anchor", "middle")` kept a tab named `middle`), and a Sheet's last tab never goes (checked on the page and by the server). The preview carries `data-ge-unit`, `data-ge-grid` (a table the table tools are for), `data-ge-lead`, `data-ge-keep="script|ref"`, `data-ge-box` and `data-ge-pure`; the map lists each unit's kind, parent unit, source hash and, when kept, the names that keep it, and each text block's innermost unit. In the page (`blocks.mjs`: `handle/`, `selection/`, `block-menu/`), moving the pointer into a unit, or onto the empty space beside it (see "Selecting blocks"), left of the block's middle line (right of it, its 批注 button: "Annotations" below), shows a handle left of its first line (its first words or picture, or its first text block left empty: a disclosure with no title has its handle on its title row, `emptyLine` in `handle/`), 10px left of the block: a grip, 18×24px, six dots in two columns of three in its quietest icon gray (`--ge-ico3`, `--c-icoTer`), no type icon; the dots drawn at the icons' 20px (2.5px dots, 5px apart across and 6px down: the user, 2026-10-26), the "+" the editor's own plus at 20px with 1.32px lines, 12.8px across (both the editor's own drawings: tests/unit/own-icons.mjs). On a page the editor edits, its side gutters are at least the room the handle takes and a little (64px, `--ge-edge-room`: the kit's `--doc-page-gutter` and `--doc-wide-gutter` point to it), from the page's first paint (`page/margin.mjs` `MARGIN_STYLE`, in the head the edit service serves: "The outline and the right side"), so on a narrow window the handle stands in the margin, clear of the text; a window wide enough for the reading column changes nothing. With the pointer on the grip it takes the hover gray (`--ge-hover`, 4px round; it keeps it while pressed and dragged), and a tooltip says it drags and opens the menu. No "+" stands beside the grip (the user's, 2026-10-26): while the grip is filled, a bar of the grip's own fill (`--ge-hover`, 4px wide, three quarters of the grip's height and centred on it, 3px clear of its fill; the user's, 2026-10-26) stands left of it, in a 24×24 square (52px left of the block), all of which reaches the bar; the pointer moving on to it turns the bar into a "+" (the editor's plus, gray, no fill) and takes the grip's fill away, and back on the grip the "+" is the bar again and the grip filled (`data-over` on the handle: "grip" or "add"). The bar takes the pointer only while the grip is filled or the "+" shows, so a pointer coming from the left never finds a "+"; it is not there while the grip is pressed; the grip never moves for it. A click on the "+" (tooltip 「点击在下方添加块」, Alt+click above) makes an empty block at the block's level right after it (Alt: right before it; a text block beside the block in its parent), puts the caret in it and opens the "/" menu there unfiltered, as a "/" typed would (`slash-menu/` `add`: the empty block says 输入以筛选… while nothing is typed, on the light gray of a temporary input (`data-ge-adding`: `--ge-field`, `--ca-graBacPriTra`, no outline round it, in `--ge-tx3`; the user, 2026-10-26), a list item nothing; what is typed then filters it, a block chosen is what the empty one turns into, its own type chosen with nothing typed changing nothing and recording no undo step); one undo step takes the block away (`addNear`: `insert/` for a paragraph where the block's box takes one — the page, a section, a column, a quote, a disclosure's content, the blocks a list item holds, a node's content — beside a list's item a paragraph at the item's level, after all it holds, in the box its list stands in, its list split there as turning the item into 文本 splits it (the items after it a list of their own, a numbered one's from 1 again): a text block beside the item in its parent (the user, 2026-10-26; an item only where that box takes no paragraph), else beside the first block out whose box takes a paragraph; a flowchart's node gets a node beside it, `flowchart/` `addAfter`, its title the menu's, whose blocks go below the title; scenario `handleBar`). The handle steps aside after the click, and a double click's second press at the same spot (within 500ms) does nothing (it would land on the page under it and take the caret out of the new block). Alt pressed alone types nothing, so it never hides the handle (a modifier key alone is no typing, `editor.mjs`); while the block menu is open (`data-menu` on the handle: the menu opens left of the grip, over the bar's square) there is no bar and no "+". A Sheet area's "+" (its handle on its tab row) makes a paragraph after the area. The block menu's "在下方新建" offers the same blocks (see "New blocks and conversions"); the strip between the handle and the unit keeps it, and in the gap between two units of a box the nearer one within 10px wins (in a tab row, the nearer tab). In edit mode the tab row's gap widens to 38px so each tab's handle fits before it; the Sheet shell itself is reached through "选中外层" from a block in a panel, since its handle would stand where the first tab's does. The handle follows the pointer only: a selected block shows its fill, not a handle that stays when the pointer is elsewhere, and crossing the gap between two blocks keeps the handle where it is until the block beside the pointer is found (never a blink). Typing, or scrolling by the wheel or a touchpad, hides the handle until the pointer moves (not the move the browser makes up when the page stops under a still pointer; a scroll the page makes itself leaves it; a section's bar stays). The handle and the menu are drawn in the editor's own layer (inside an open modal dialog while there is one), never inside the document's DOM. A selected unit is filled (see "Selecting blocks", which also covers several selected at once). Clicking the handle (or the right button) selects the unit (text focus and selection are dropped, so keys go to the page) and opens the block menu (`block-menu/`, on the card of `menu.mjs`: ↑/↓ and Enter forwarded from the page, ←/→ along its grid of types, Esc or a second click closes it): the types the block can be turned into as icons side by side (see "New blocks and conversions"), layout rows for cards and tables, "在下方新建一块", "分区与分栏 ›" and 复制块链接 right above deleting (one row for both, its submenu as wide as the menu: "在下方新建分区", "组成新分区" or "解散分区", then "分两栏", "分三栏", "分四栏", then "选中外层", the unit around it, each group after a line; see "Columns"), "删除…" (disabled, with the reason, for a kept unit) and, right below it, "选中外层" for a unit that has no such submenu (a list item, a flowchart's node, a quote's later paragraph, a block held under an item). A block straight in a detail card has no "选中外层": what stands behind the card is not around it (the box's `outer: false`). Esc in text selects the unit around the caret (not inside a modal dialog, whose Esc closes it). With a unit selected, Delete or Backspace deletes it, arrows select the neighbouring unit in the same box, Enter puts the caret at the end of the text of the first one selected (a formula block's editor opens: its page object's `activate`), or, when that one is no text block (a code block, a highlight block, a disclosure, a table, a card, a divider, a row of columns: those a click to their right puts the caret after, "The caret after a block"; a 选项卡区块: `entersAfter()` in `page/beside.mjs`, beside `caretBeside()`, reading the definitions' `boxed`), puts an empty paragraph right after the last one with the caret in it, Esc clears the selection; a click elsewhere clears it too. Deleting removes the unit with its leads through the editor's structural undo step, which selects it again on undo. `removalTarget()` climbs from an emptied unit to its box when the box is itself a pure unit that is not kept (a list, quote or plain div, a column), so the same rule serves Backspace in an emptied paragraph; kept units never go that way either, nor merge away. New blocks not saved yet have a handle too; deleting one just drops it. Deleting a tab also drops its panel, and when that Sheet was shown, the next tab is clicked (undo clicks the restored tab again). A section has no handle at its left (its heading is a block of its own, with its own handle): with the pointer at its right side (from its right edge out 28px, along its height; not on what floats over the page there, the format bar and its menus, a menu, the link and formula cards, a media block's bar, the icon picker, a 批注's card, `handle/` `FLOATING`: it lit up under them until 2026-11-17) a light grey bar spans it (4px in a 16px strip 6px right of it; colors from `light-dark()`, darker under the pointer, the accent while the section is selected), and the bar is its handle: a click selects the section and opens its menu right of the bar, a press moved more than 4px drags it. A Sheet area has none. A press on an `<hr>` (anywhere on its band, 12px above and below the line, not over text being edited) selects it at once. `TOGGLES` (`components/registry.mjs`, from each component's description) lists the classes a unit kind may switch; `collectEdits()` sends each switch as `{ id, toggle, on }`, and `applyEdits()` rewrites only the `class` attribute of the unit's start tag (content changes do not matter). `ATTRIBUTES` lists the attributes a unit kind may set, each with the test of its values (a code block's `data-language`); each change goes as `{ id, attribute, value }` (`value` null removes it) and `applyEdits()` sets, replaces or removes that attribute alone in the start tag. On saving, `collectEdits()` documents deleted units, outermost only (an element out of the document with no parent), as `units: [{ id, remove: true }]`, and no text edit for text blocks whose innermost unit went with them. `commitEdits()` finds each deleted unit (it is gone already if the file no longer has it and nothing takes its place); `applyEdits()` refuses kept units, deletes units next to each other (only whitespace between) as one run that takes its lines and one neighbouring blank line along (the one after it, else the one before), and drops text edits inside a deleted run. A deletion the file's version of the unit does not agree with (the unit changed since the page saw it) is a conflict carrying `unit` instead of `id`, which a commit holds back for the reader ("Conflicts"; `force` deletes the file's version). A folded heading selected goes with what its fold hides (or as the section it covers whole), said 已删除标题和折叠的 N 块, one undo step that puts it all back folded ("Folding a heading's section").
- **One document of flows.** For editing, the document is one document: the page (`main`) and each Sheet panel hold body text and chapters (sections and Sheet areas) side by side, as the file has them, and every one of those is a flow of blocks. A section (分区) is optional: it groups blocks (its heading and a divider line at its top are blocks in it like any other) that move, go or dissolve together; a document may be written without any, and a heading never makes one by itself (see "Sections"). Boxes have a role (`boxRole()` in `components/registry.mjs`): `root` (main, panels: chapters and body text), `flow` (sections, panels, disclosure and dialog contents, plain divs), `column` (a column of a row: a flow that takes no row of columns), `columns` (a row of columns), `lines` (the header), `quote`, `row` (grids), `list`, `nest` (a list item, which takes lists) and `tabs`; `accepts(boxKind, unitKind)` says what goes where, and the page and the server use the same function. The page and a panel take chapters (sections, Sheet areas, custom blocks, detail cards made on the page) and every block a flow takes; a flow takes every block but chapters, tabs and list items; the header and a quote take paragraphs; a column takes every block a flow takes but a row of columns; a row of columns takes its columns; a list takes its items; a tab row only its own tabs. The opening summary (`.doc-summary`) is a highlight panel like any other: it moves, goes and holds what a panel holds; only the writing rules put it first. A `<footer>` is a block like any other. This works because the kit keeps three promises (see "Flows" under Design defaults): body text looks the same in every flow, a component looks the same in every flow, and formulas and code render in every flow. So a move is always "put this block next to that one": the editor never creates, splits or merges a wrapper, and a plain div inside a section is just part of the section's flow (rows of columns are the one wrapper the editor makes and dissolves, see "Columns").
- **Moving blocks.** Pressing a unit's handle and moving the pointer more than 4px drags it (pointer capture on the handle, which stays in the page for the whole drag, even while the dragged block is in a Sheet no longer shown): the unit and its lead fade to half (`data-ge-dragging`), a faded copy of each dragged block follows the pointer (`drag/preview.mjs`: each copy where its block stands when the drag starts, moving by as much as the pointer moves; at most 12, each at most 60% of the window tall, cut with a fade; an ordered list's items keep their numbers; the copies live in a shadow root, out of sight of the editor's map, the commit's scans, the kit's scripts and the page's ids, with the page's rules brought in as one stylesheet and each copy in a frame of its ancestors, `display: contents`, so it looks as on the page; a frame draws nothing of its own, `visibility: hidden` with the copy `visible`, since `display: contents` still draws an element's `::before`/`::after` and a `<details>`'s summary: Chrome's own "▶ 详细信息" for a frame's summary-less disclosure, an ancestor item's marker, a flowchart step's number, each at the window's edge following the pointer (scenario `dragPreviewFrames`)); when the drag ends the copy lands: it travels from where the pointer left it to where the pressed block stands now and fades into it, 160ms `cubic-bezier(.2, .8, .2, 1)`, keeping most of its .4 on the way (.32 three quarters of it) and fading out as it comes onto the block, then goes (`land`, the owner's pick of 2026-11-17): dropped, onto the block in its new place, the page scrolled since counted; cancelled (Esc, the focus lost) or released where it was, back to the block where it stands; at once with reduced motion or when the block shows nowhere; only the picture moves, never a block of the page, and the move and its undo step are as they were; the landing copy is an overlay of its own, `drag landing`, the pressed block's, until it goes), and a 3px light blue line (`rgb(151 197 241)`) shows where it would go. Where it goes is `resolveDrop()` in `drag/drop.mjs`, a pure function of `measure()`'s tree (`geometry.mjs`) (measured again at most every 100ms while dragging, from the content of the detail card the dragged block is in when there is one; tested in `tests/unit/geometry.mjs`): the page's margins count as the page (the pointer's x taken to the page's edge); the box is the innermost one around the pointer that accepts every block dragged and is not inside one of them; the place is the gap between two of its blocks nearest the pointer (by their middles), a vertical line between side-by-side blocks (a tab row, a flowchart's nodes, from the box's computed style) or at the end of the row the pointer is in (in a box whose rows wrap, a flowchart's, `sideBySide()` "wrap": a block alone in its row, a node holding a picture or one of a 纵向 flowchart, takes the places above and below it by its middle height, the line across it between the rows, the pointer between its row and the next one too), and a line along the bottom of an empty box. How deep a drop goes follows each container's own layout. Containers with edges (sections, highlight panels, disclosures, Sheet panels) go by those edges: inside one, its places; past its edge, the container around it (between two sections, the gap between them belongs to the page or the Sheet; a section adds no spacing of its own, the gap is its blocks'). Where edges coincide (a section's last highlight panel ends where the section does; the last section of a Sheet panel ends where the panel and the Sheet area do), the containers flush with the block beside the place form a stack: the innermost is reached from inside, and past the edge each further 12px leaves one more of them (at most 36px out), so every one of those places can be reached. Lists go by indentation, as their items show it: at a list's end, left of its markers (20px past where its items start, so the marker column still counts as the list) the blocks leave it for the list around it, after the item holding it (and do not go back under that item); a step (24px) right of the text of the item above the place they go under that item, at the end of its last list or in a new list of the dragged item's kind, the line starting at that item's text (where the markers of a list in it stand; between items it starts at the list's edge, with their markers); just below lists that end together, the deepest whose items start left of the pointer.
