Blocks of other kinds (a paragraph, an image…) go under an item too (`held` in `drop.mjs`): among the blocks it holds, below its own line (its box for them taking them then), or a step right of the text of the item above the place (over a list, or right after one; the deepest so reached), at the end of what it holds, the line along the bottom of that item from its text (scenario `listHeldBlocks`). The container the blocks would go into is tinted (`rgb(35 131 226 / 7%)`, 2px past it; none for the page itself); there is no label naming it (the user found it noise: the line and the tint say where, and the status line names where they went). The status line says how many blocks move. Over a tab row nothing drops; resting on a tab for 450ms switches to that Sheet, so a block can go into another Sheet, and resting on the title of a closed disclosure opens it, so a block can go in (disclosures opened on the way close again unless the block went into one). Near the top or bottom of the window the page scrolls, or, in a detail card, the content of the card the gesture started in (the one on top when one was opened from another, never the card under it) (`edge-scroll.mjs`: one rule for a drag, a box selection and a selection across blocks, the last two tasks of the editor's frames; what scrolls is the gesture's: `edgeScroll(x, y, scroller)`). Releasing moves it there (nothing happens where it already was), Esc cancels. Ctrl+Shift+↑/↓ (←/→ as well for a selected block) move it by one place within its box; in text, Ctrl+Shift+↑/↓ moves the block around the caret and keeps the caret. Blocks made on the page move like any other: they are written where they end up. The page moves the very same nodes (`moveBefore()` where the browser has it, which keeps their state, else `insertBefore()`), so charts, tables and disclosures stay as they are; a container the block leaves empty goes too when it is a pure, unkept unit (as when its last block is deleted: an emptied list, quote or plain div, a column (and a row left with one column gives its blocks back: "Columns")); a moved Sheet tab leaves its panel where it is (its area starts again: "Tabs"). It is one structural undo step (over the old and the new container) that keeps the block selected both ways. `captureLayout()` records each box's unit order; `collectEdits()` compares it with the page box by box (longest common subsequence: the longest run still in order stays) and sends every other unit there, moved within the box or from another one, as `{ id, move: { after } }` next to the unit that stayed before it, `{ before }` the one after it, or `{ into: box }` when no original unit of the box stayed, in page order. `applyEdits()` checks the target (`accepts`, not inside the unit, a tab in its own row), takes the unit out like a deleted one and inserts it after (or before) the anchor, set apart by the whitespace between two neighbouring units of the target box, or before the box's end tag (`into`), with its lines after the first moved from the old indentation to the new one (lines inside `<pre>`, `<textarea>`, `<script>` and `<style>` stay). Everything inside a moved unit (text edits, deletions, class switches, units moved within it) is applied to the text that moves, even when the unit leaves a wrapper that is deleted; units moved or deleted next to each other leave as one run. Leaving takes a neighbouring blank line only between two blank lines or at the start or end of the container, so a blank line that separates groups of blocks stays. A folded heading dragged takes what its fold hides along (`fold/` `takenWith`: the same nodes, one undo step, still folded after it and after its undo; dragged beside a block into a row of columns too), or the section it covers whole ("Folding a heading's section"); the faded copy shows only what is in sight (the heading), and the status line says 标题和折叠的 N 块. Dropped below a folded heading, blocks land in its section and open it (the rule that opens a fold); at a container's end the place is after what the last block in sight hides (`lastOf`), never between a folded heading and its section. Ctrl+Shift+↑/↓ move a folded heading alone a section of its level at a time (`stepOver`: down past the next block, which ends its section, and what follows that up to the next block ending a section of its level, so an h2 never lands between the next chapter's heading and its words; up back to the block before it that ends one, or to the box's start), and other blocks, several selected too, a block in sight at a time, a folded heading and what it hides counting as one (`groupsOf`). Files and pictures dragged in from outside the page show and land by the same rules ("Dropping in from outside").
- **Dropping in from outside.** `drop-in/` (the user's of 2026-11-20: files, pictures and videos dragged in from the computer, and pictures dragged from another web page, did nothing). Before it a drop in a block's text was prevented and nothing else was looked at: let go on a block's words nothing happened, and anywhere else (the margins, an empty image, a card, the workbench's sidebar, a presented page) Chrome opened the file, or a link's page, in a tab of its own. What it takes, by what can be read of a drag while it is over the page (its types and its items' kinds and types; the data waits for the drop): a drag that carries `Files` (from the system's file manager or the desktop, another program, a picture another page lets Chrome carry as a file), and a picture of another page, Chrome's drag of an `<img>`, which carries its address (`text/uri-list`) and its markup (`text/html`) and no `text/plain` (a link's and selected words' drags carry plain text; Firefox's picture says `application/x-moz-nativeimage`). While it is over the page the place shows as for blocks dragged by their handle (drag/'s `outside`, the same `resolveDrop`, line, tint, edge scrolling, resting on a Sheet tab or a closed disclosure's title; no faded copy, no handle): the kinds it would make are what the page objects make of those files (`acceptsFile`, `forFile`: a picture an image, any other file a file block), so it goes between blocks, into a highlight block, a column, a disclosure, a custom block's slot or a section by their edges, under a list item, beside a block into a new row of columns, before or after a table (a cell takes pictures only pasted at its caret: "Pictures in a cell"), and over the page's title to the page's first place. Over an empty placeholder that takes it (`takesDrop`: an image's for a picture file or a picture of a page, a file block's for any file, an embed's for an HTML or PDF file, as their 上传 menus take; not one a custom block keeps) that placeholder is tinted and no line shows (the media menu, `media-menu/`, takes a file dropped on it too). The drop: each file is made the block that file pasted makes (`forFile`: the block, not on the page yet, and its fill), in the order they came, all put where the line was in one structural undo step (`outside.put`: placed as a block dragged there is, `selectAfter` them on redo) and selected, then each filled (its upload, `media.place` `pasted`: `finishStep`, one step with the drop; the others stay selected as each fills); one undo takes them all off. Gallery has no block of its own for a video or a sound: they are file blocks, as a pasted one is (20 MB at most, the upload's limit for a file). A file too big makes no block and says the paste's words (`refusesFile`: 图片太大（最多 10 MB）。, 文件太大（最多 20 MB）。); a folder (`webkitGetAsEntry().isDirectory`, read in the drop) makes none either and says so (文件夹不能放进文档：…, or, dropped with files, 文件夹不能放进文档，只放进了和它一起拖进来的文件。): Gallery has no folder block. On a placeholder the first file it takes goes in it (`takeDrop`, its upload; one step with the others, which go right after it, `{ after }`). A picture of another page is read off the drop's markup: its one `<img>` with no words beside it (an `<img>` in a link carries the link's address in `text/uri-list`, so the markup is what names the picture), else an address that names a picture; http(s) or a picture's `data:` address, made an image there (`forPicture`) whose picture the server downloads into the document's folder, as a picture pasted with its address (`fromLink`), or put in the empty image it is let go on. What else is dragged in (words, a link, words and pictures of a page together) and anything dragged from this page itself (its selected words: a `dragstart` in the page) is not taken: let go in a block's text, nothing goes in (as before; and `insertFromDrop` is refused), and where nothing takes it the browser opens nothing (`shared/drop-guard.mjs`). The drag is over 200ms after its last `dragover` (the browser sends one every few dozen ms while a drag stays over the page). `shared/drop-guard.mjs` `guardDrops(window)`: a drag of files or of a link (`text/uri-list`) that no part of a page took (`defaultPrevented`: the editor's blocks, the media menu, the import dialog's drop zone, a block's own) is refused there (`dropEffect` none, the drop prevented), words and links into a field that takes text left to it; it runs in the workbench (`main.tsx`) and, as an inline script, on every page the server serves of a document (edited, presented, a version: `routes.mjs` `dropGuardScript`) and on a custom page. An exported file has none (the kit unchanged): there Chrome still opens what is let go in a new tab, the offline page staying as it was, which holds nothing unsaved. Scenarios `dropFiles`, `dropWebPicture`, `dropFilesInContainers`, `dropFilesElsewhere`.
- **Selecting blocks.** `geometry.mjs`, `selection/` (`marquee.mjs`), `handle/`. A highlight block's icon is no block a box selection picks: its column, left of the block's lines, counts as part of each line, so a box drawn down it takes those lines (`pick()`). `measure()` reads the page into a tree of boxes and units with their rectangles in page coordinates (what is not shown is left out; given a band, only the units reaching into it). A closed disclosure's content is no part of the tree (`folded()`): the browser still places its blocks, under the title and over the blocks after the disclosure, where a box selection or the pointer would otherwise find them. A unit's rectangle is where the reader sees it (`extentOf()`, which the handle and the fills use as well): a list item's reaches left over its marker to its list's edge, so the marker is filled with it and the handle stands clear of it; a divider line's is a band 12px above and below the line, as tall as the handle, so its fill is more than a sliver and the pointer keeps its handle on the way to it (a divider at a section's top reaches past the section: `hoverAt()` and `pick()` look into every block, and a drop's line before a box's first block or after its last is measured from no further out than the box's edge); where an item's text starts is kept beside it for the indentation rules of a drop; `hoverAt()`, `pick()`, `endOf()` and `innerBox()` are pure functions of that tree (`tests/unit/geometry.mjs`): geometry proposes, the boxes and units decide the level, `accepts()` and the block rules decide what may happen. *The handle beside empty space*: with the pointer on empty space (`blankTarget()`: the page around the document, or a box between its blocks; never text, though a list item is also the box of its lists), the handle is that of the block whose rectangle, widened by 150px on both sides, holds the pointer, the one nested deepest (an item rather than its list, a paragraph rather than its section), of blocks side by side the nearest; sections and Sheet areas count only through their blocks. *The handle follows the pointer's height*: in a block holding blocks (a list item and its lists, a quote, a disclosure, a section's or a column's space), on its blocks or beside them, it stands by the innermost block at the pointer's height (`atHeight()`), in a gap between blocks by the nearer one by height (then by width), and by the outer block only on its own head (a list item's own line, a title above what it holds); the strip between a handle and its block keeps that handle, on the way to it, only at the heights where it is that block's, so moving left and down from a list item's line by its nested items gives theirs, never the outer item's first line. *Box selection*: a mouse press on empty space (not on an edge that resizes, nor in a gap that does, between two columns or two nodes of a flowchart's row; while a detail card is open, only on the empty space of its content, see "Detail cards") moved more than 4px draws a rectangle (`rgb(35 131 226 / 14%)`) whose start stays on the content while the page scrolls, by the wheel or near the window's top or bottom edge as for a drag; text selection is off while the press lasts and the handle steps aside. The press is not cancelled, so the page takes the focus as a click on empty space always gave it (in a workbench, its keys then reach the page). The editor's own controls (the handle, its menu, the format bar) cancel their press to keep the caret where it is, so a press on them moves no focus; when the page does not have the focus (in a workbench, a document just opened from the sidebar), the press gives it to the page (`window.focus()`), or the keys after it, Ctrl+Z among them, would go to the workbench. What the rectangle takes (`pick()`): the units it touches that hold no other unit it touches, each lifted to the child of the innermost box around all of them; a list item whose own line it touches (measure()'s `own`, above the lists it holds) counts as one of those, its lists with it, so the rectangle takes the item whole once it reaches that line and the nested items it touches when it does not, whichever side it was drawn from. Two paragraphs of a section give those paragraphs, a paragraph in each of two sections the two sections, items of a list the items, an item and the paragraph before its list that paragraph and the list, a Sheet tab and a paragraph of its panel the Sheet area; a box standing loose in that box, no block of its own (the document's header in the page, around its lines), is looked into, the lines it touches taken with the page's blocks as they stand in it: a box from the header's small text (the opening line an Agent writes under the title) into the opening summary takes that line and the summary, drawn down or up, touching the line by a pixel or across all of it (0.3.0 left the header's lines out and took the summary alone; scenario `headerBoxSelect`); the title, no block, is never taken, nor is the page's icon above it, a unit of the header that is never selected (its unit's `selectable: false`, `isSelectable()` in `registry.mjs`; the icon's own controls are its tools): a box passes over it as over a highlight block's icon, `select()` leaves it out whoever asks (Esc after a selection across blocks, Ctrl+A, an undo putting blocks back), the keys below pass over it and Ctrl+Shift+arrows move no line of the header past it (until 2026-11-19 a box over it, or ↑ from the header's first line, selected it, and Delete took it off). Shift, held at any moment, adds what it takes to the selection there was when the press started or takes it out of it, so a selection may hold blocks of several boxes. *The selection* is a set of units, none inside another, in document order (`select()` takes one or several); each is filled in the editor's layer with the same blue, reaching past it (`reachOf()` in `overlay-layer.mjs`): 2px around text, 4px round; 6px around a block that shows a box of its own (a background or a border, rounded: a code block, a highlight block, a card; `boxRadius()`; never the editor's tint over it, an inset shadow, so the pointer changes no fill), its corners the block's and 6px more, so the two are concentric; a block whose square box says how far its fill reaches (`--doc-box-reach`, a callout's 6px) is round by as much. Above and below it, one rule for every kind of block, no two neighbours' fills overlap (`roomOf()`): a fill reaches toward the block next to it that way (its nearest unit over or under it in its container, else its container's) at most half the gap less a pixel left between them ((gap − 1) / 2, rounded down), or what that block's own reach leaves of the gap less a pixel when that is more, and the fill reaches as far above as below, the smaller of its two rooms (`reachOf`), so it is even and a run of blocks is filled alike, the first and the last as the ones between: two disclosures or code blocks 8px apart are filled 3px all round, a hairline (1–2px) between the fills, while one standing alone 12px from the paragraphs around it keeps its 6px; list items 5px apart keep their 2px. A block of its own box is filled as evenly at its sides (never 6px at the sides beside 3 above and below) and its corners are that much less round, a circle (the block's own and that reach: 13px for a disclosure filled 3px), never an ellipse; a block of text keeps its 2px at its sides. The rule is the blocks', not the selection's: a block selected alone is filled as in a group, and the frame of what is edited stands where its fill would. A paragraph or heading given a background is text all the same: the kit lays its fill 2px past it, 4px round (a spread shadow, taking no room, `base.css`), just where its selection's light blue goes (decided 2026-10-16), so giving one moves nothing. A list item's marker is included. A divider line is a line, no box: its fill is a band around the line (`fillExtentOf()`: 14px, 2px more each side), while the band the pointer finds it by stays 24px. The fills are hidden while the blocks are dragged. The menu of any of them ("已选中 N 块"; from the handle of a block inside one of them too, an item of a selected list: `holderOf`) offers below its title the types they can all be turned into, as one block's grid (see "New blocks and conversions"), then 颜色 when they all take one, 分区与分栏 and 删除; blocks of two containers (the header's lines with the page's blocks) have no row of 分区与分栏 (its rows go with blocks of one box), so the menu has none. Delete or Backspace, or its 删除, removes them all as one undo step, which selects them again on undo; when one of them may not go (kept, or the last tabs of a row) none goes, and the reason names it. `removalTarget(element, gone)` counts the others going as gone, so a list whose items all go goes too. Dragging the handle of any of them moves them all, in their order, into a box that accepts every one of them (tabs only within their row); Ctrl+Shift+arrows move them when they stand next to each other in one box (blocks of several containers, the header's lines with the page's blocks, stay where they are, saying 选中的块不在同一层 and that a drag moves them: dragged, they all go into the box dropped in, so the header's lines leave the header as small text of the page); Shift+↑/↓ widen or narrow the selection among the neighbours of the block it started from; ↑/↓ select the block before the first or after the last; for both, a box standing loose in another (the header in the page) has its blocks where it stands among that box's, one run (`runOf()` in `selection/`): ↓ from the header's last line is the page's first block, ↑ from the page's first block the header's last line, and from the header's first line ↑ and Shift+↑ go no further (the page's icon is no block of the run); Esc, a click on empty space or into text clears it. *A click on empty space* below the last block of the box clicked (the page, which the space below the document belongs to, a Sheet panel, a section, a detail card's content, a flowchart's node: the flows, a column; never the header, a quote, a list or a row of columns) starts a paragraph at the end of that box with the caret in it, or, when the last block, followed down through the flows it holds (a highlight block's and a disclosure's too), is an empty paragraph, puts the caret there instead (`endOf()`), so clicking again makes no second one; a section that has ended does not take it, the page does. Under the last block of a box whose end is its own padding (its description's `endIsPadding`: a highlight block, the opening summary, a disclosure's content) a click makes no block (the user's, 2026-11-06: one made there by a slip of the pointer was saved): it is a gap like any other (below), the caret into that last block when it is text. A node keeps it, stretched to its row's height as a column is; an empty content's placeholder and a highlight block of only its icon still take a paragraph on a click ("Empty blocks"). Elsewhere on empty space (`insert/` `clickBlank`): to the right of a boxed block (a code block, a callout, a highlight block, a disclosure, a table, a card, a formula block, a divider, an instance of a custom block…: `BOXED`) or of a row of columns (`caretBeside()`), at its height, from its own right edge out (`boxedBeside()`: between a block narrower than its text area, a table, a picture, a card or embed of a set width, and that area's edge; in the padding of the highlight block or callout around it; in the page's margin), the caret goes after that block, at its bottom right, and nothing is selected ("The caret after a block"; the user's, 2026-11-09: such a click selected the block before, from 2026-11-07 only past the text area, a click between a narrower block and the area's edge going to the nearest text); of several the click is right of, the innermost (a table in a highlight block, by a click in the page's margin), but never a block in a row of columns the click is right of too (the caret goes after the row); nor does a click in one text area take a block standing in another (the blank box pressed, `target`, holds the block: a click in a column takes nothing of the column left of it, the block right of which it is in its own column). Past a column's edge is the gap to the next column, whose press is the gap's (it drags it; a click alone changes nothing, a double click evens the columns: "Columns"); beside text, the caret goes into the text block on that line, nearest the click (at a wrapped line's end, that line's, not the next one's start: `selectAt()` in `caret.mjs`, as a press beside a line of a block's own text takes it, `besideLine()`); in the gap between two blocks, or beside a block of no text, into the nearer of the blocks around it that is text (its last line from below, its first from above), in the innermost box at that height (`innerBox()`: a press on the page's margin is the page's, the block it stands beside a Sheet panel's, a section's, a disclosure's, a column's), else in the boxes around that one up to the box clicked (in a highlight block whose last block is no text, nowhere), and the page stays where it is, that text out of sight too (`placeCaret()` in `caret.mjs`: a key typed there brings it); and a second click there right after the first (a double click: the press on empty space is the box selection's, so the browser makes no `dblclick`) between two boxed blocks makes a paragraph between them, with the caret. Elsewhere, beside a text block that is a unit of its own (a paragraph, heading or list item) on that line, a click puts the caret into it where that line is nearest the click (`hoverAt()` over text blocks at any distance, then `nearestOnLine()` in `caret.mjs`: the point taken onto that line of the block's own text, or the nearest line). A press inside a text block beside one of its lines (right of a line that ends early) gets the caret the browser gives it, except where the browser puts it elsewhere on that line: after a list item's text followed by its lists (not editable), Chrome puts it at the line's start, and the editor puts it at the nearest place of that line instead (`besideLine()`, a mouse press only there, so dragging from beside a line still selects text elsewhere); anywhere else a click on empty space only clears the selection. The paragraph is written once it has text. What a folded heading hides is never selected (`select()` leaves it out, a box drawn measures only what shows, Shift+↑/↓ and ↑/↓ pass over it); what is taken away whole takes it along when it acts ("Folding a heading's section"), so deleting a selected folded heading deletes its section.
- **Selecting text across blocks.** `text-select/` (each block is a host of its own, and Chrome keeps a selection inside one, so a selection across blocks is the editor's own). A mouse press in a block's text starts as the browser's selection; once the pointer leaves that block, the page takes it over: from where the press began to the text under the pointer (`pointAt()`: the text block under it, else the nearest by height, then by width for blocks side by side; above a block its start, below it its end), drawn as the browser draws a selection, a line high so its lines meet (a highlight of the CSS Custom Highlight API is only as high as the letters and leaves a gap between lines): a strip over each line of each block's selected text, from its first letter to its last, in a layer of its own at the page's origin (`.ge-cross-layer`, scrolling with the page, drawn again when the window resizes) blended over the page (multiplied on a light page, screened on a dark one) so the text keeps its color, the strips registered overlays (`cross-selection`); its ranges kept as the highlight `ge-cross` (transparent: what it holds), and in a modal dialog, which that layer is under, drawn by the highlight `ge-cross-shown`; meanwhile the browser's selecting is held off (`data-ge-cross` on the root: `user-select: none`); back in the block it began in, the browser's selection again. Its text is body text's, headings' and list items' (in quotes, highlight blocks, columns…: text blocks of kind `flow`), and the document's title's (`isTitle`: a selection runs from the title into the blocks and back; the user's of 2026-11-09), a block holding one of its ends only as far as its own text is inside it (an item whose list a selection begins in has no piece of its own text: `piecesOf`, so the format bar turns or formats only the items); a block holding none (a code block, a table, a file, an image, a bookmark, an embed, a formula block, a divider…: `wholeOf()`) is taken whole as it passes it, filled as a selected block is without being selected (`selection/` `mark`, the same fill) and its own text not drawn; over one, an end stands before it or after it, whichever leaves it inside (`edge()`), and a press begun in one (its code, a cell, a file's row) takes it whole once the pointer has left it, the block's own behaviour staying while the pointer is in it. Deleting takes these blocks with the rest (scenario `crossSelectWhole`). A tabs area's tabs are no blocks of it (`ownPart`), nor are the blocks of a tab not shown: passed over, they are neither drawn, copied nor deleted (an area it holds all of goes whole). Near the window's top or bottom edge the page scrolls as long as the pointer stays there. Shift+click in another block draws it from the caret. Selected text looks the same either way, whatever the system's colors: a light blue (`rgb(35 131 226 / 28%)`, 45% dark, as the link box's stand-in), the text keeping its color. It stays after the press: Ctrl+C copies it as blocks, each of its type, the first and the last partly (see "Copying text"), Ctrl+X copies and deletes it (a cut holding a card, or taking whole a block the page cannot make, a whole block, is refused and deletes nothing, as a cut of blocks is: its copy would be its words, which put no block of it back); Backspace, Delete, typing (a key, an input method, or text put in without a key) and a paste delete it first, Enter too before splitting: the first block keeps its text before it and takes the last block's after it (`mergeInto(…, { anyKind: true })`, any two text blocks: a list item's rest joins a paragraph, a paragraph's an item; when they cannot join, each keeps what is left), the blocks between go (`removalTarget()`, so a list they empty goes too), as one undo step, the caret at the join. From the document's title nothing joins (`apart`, the user's: 删除的话，下面的文本不会自动跑到标题里面): the title keeps what stands before the selection (all of it selected, it is left empty, saying 未命名文档), the last block what stands after it in a block of its own (gone when that is nothing, as the blocks between), the caret in the title where the selection began, so what is typed over it (a key, an input method) goes in the title; the title's change and the blocks' are committed as any, and a copy holds the title's part as a paragraph. Shift+arrow keys do not make or widen a selection across blocks (an arrow collapses it), from the title as anywhere. Only body text, headings and list items are deleted this way; with other text at either end it says so and deletes nothing (it still copies). A block between its ends goes as a selected block would (`keepText` only: a block taken whole, a formula block too; it once refused one with "这里有不能删除的块"). Once the press ends the format bar shows for it (`format-bar/` `showAcross`, by its last piece's end): a format goes on each block's piece of it (a toggle on unless every piece has it, a color the same in all of them shown as such), one undo step (`struct` with the pieces' blocks), the selection drawn again over them (`redraw`); its type row is there when it holds nothing but text (no block taken whole: every piece of it a block's text; a highlight block's icon it goes over, its box's lead, is none): the types its blocks can all be turned into (`showAcross` → `crossTurn`, the blocks it reaches text of, an empty one it goes over among them; convert.mjs `groupTargets`), named by their type when they share one and "多个类型" (by the first one's icon) for a mix; a pick turns them all as the block menu of several blocks does (below, "New blocks and conversions"), one undo step, and the selection is drawn again over their text where it is now (`rangeOffsets` before, `rangeAt` after, `reselect` → `redraw`); turned into blocks that hold no text of the body (a disclosure, a code block) they are selected instead (scenario `groupTurnInto`); the link, formula and detail card are for text in one block, their buttons off; its keys (Ctrl+B, Ctrl+I…) format it too (`formatKey`), and a press on the bar keeps it (`keeps`) (scenario `crossFormat`). Esc selects the blocks it touches (a list whose every item it reaches as the list), an arrow collapses it to its start (←/↑) or end (→/↓), Ctrl+A selects the blocks of the first container holding all it touches (see "Selecting all"), and anything else (a press, Ctrl+Z) lets it go. A chip of the text (a formula, a link shown with its icon, a page block) is one character to a selection, in a block as across blocks (a token, `user-select: all`): a drag that reaches any part of one takes all of it, the selection's ends beside it and never in its text. In a block holding chips a press is the editor's (`chipsPress`, `followPress`, `dragPlace`), for the browser's place on a chip comes and goes as the pointer passes the chip's pieces, and it would take back what a move put right (a mousemove cancelled does not stop its selecting). A chip the selection holds whole shows as one: the browser paints nothing on its pieces (`::selection` transparent), and it is filled with the selection's color, one rectangle around all of it, the room at its sides too, as high as its line as the words selected beside it are, a line a tall formula made as well (`chip-fills.mjs`: a box of its own behind it, `::after`, in a style sheet of the page's own that names the chips held, no attribute, so that nothing enters a block's snapshots or reaches the observers; written when the chips held change, 10ms on a page of a thousand blocks, nothing for a caret or words; a chip the line wraps fills its pieces); across blocks the page's strips take a strip of each chip held, over all of its drawing, and none of its pieces. A selection going on past a folded heading takes what the fold hides too, wherever in the heading's words it begins (`piecesOf`: its text blocks' text in full, its other blocks whole; an end is never in what is hidden, so what is hidden in the range lies between the ends, which deleting takes whole already, `unitsBetween`), so a copy or a cut carries the section, as a closed disclosure held whole is taken whole, and what a cut copies is what it deletes: one begun after the heading's first words copied none of the hidden paragraphs while its deletion took them (a reviewer's finding of 2026-11-06).
- **Selecting all.** `select-all/`: Ctrl+A (⌘A on a Mac, where Ctrl+A stays the line's start) widens step by step (the first press selects the text block's text, a press with it all selected, or empty, the page's blocks; with blocks selected, the page's) with the containers between, as the user asked (2026-10-23), one level a press. The first press selects the text block's own text, the browser's selection, so the format bar shows for it (a list item's words, not the blocks it holds; a code block's code; a cell's; a disclosure's title; the page's title). The next selects, as blocks are selected (their fills, Delete, Ctrl+C, Esc as for any selection), everything in the container block it stands in: a highlight block's blocks (not its icon, the box's lead), a disclosure's content (not its title, the disclosure's own text), a quote's paragraphs, a column's blocks, the blocks of the tab shown, a detail card's content, and the blocks a list item holds under its text (its own words not among them). Each press after it the next container up, the one just selected now one of its blocks; a row of columns, a Sheet area and a table are a step of their own, the block whole (all its columns, all its tabs, all its cells), above a column, a tab's panel and a cell (from a cell: its text, then the table, then the table's container). Last the page: every block of it, the header's lines and the page's blocks (not its title or its icon, the page's own and no blocks); in an open detail card its content is the top, never the page behind it, and with a card opened from another's it is the card the press is in (the text block's or the selected blocks', else the one holding the focus, else the one drawn over the others: `openCard`), never the one under it. Sections are no step (the user's: 分区的不用管; a section's blocks are the page's to select), nor are lists (an item is a block of the flow it stands in), the plain divs that group blocks, the header and a Sheet's tab row. A press that would select nothing more than is selected goes on to the next step (`coverage()`: the text blocks reached, the blocks holding none, and a highlight block itself, whose box makes it more than its blocks; a list and its items, a quote and its paragraphs reach the same), so a paragraph alone in a highlight block goes from its text to what holds the highlight block; the top is never passed over after a text block's text, though it holds that block alone (a page or a detail card of one paragraph: its text, then the paragraph as a block); at the top a press does nothing. Blocks already selected (a box selection, a handle's, Esc's) go on from the innermost box around them, selected cells (`table/`) from their table whole (the cells let go), a selection across blocks (`text-select/`) from the first container holding all it touches (its text being only a part of theirs, that container even when it selects no more blocks), and with nothing selected and no caret, the page. The status line says what each press selected and what the next one would (`已选中这一块的文字 · 再按 Ctrl+A 选中高亮块里的全部内容`; the key named as the tooltips name it, `shortcut("mod+a")`: ⌘A on a Mac). A field of the page's own UI keeps the browser's Ctrl+A: the link card's, the formula editor's, a menu's search field, a list number's, a tab being renamed (its name), and a document's own editable element (the workbench's 写作偏好 and search are the workbench's document, never the page's). Scenario `selectAllSteps`; the steps in jsdom in `tests/unit/editor.mjs`. A container's blocks selected leave out what folded headings hide; deleting or copying them takes it along by the folded headings.
- **The caret after a block.** `after/` (the user's, 2026-11-09: until then a click there selected the block before). A click right of a block of no text of its own ("Selecting blocks": `boxedBeside()`) puts a caret after it, at its bottom right: an empty field of the editor's own layer (`overlay-layer.mjs`: the page's, or an open dialog's), never the report's, focused, so the browser draws its caret there and blinks it as in any text, in the color of the body text; in the font of the box the block stands in, its line as tall as a line of that box's text, 1px right of the block's extent (`extentOf()`: a divider's band) with its bottom on the block's, and kept there as the page moves (a task of `frames.mjs`, `FOLLOW`). Nothing is selected meanwhile, and the handle and the menus are as they are beside any caret. Backspace and Delete turn the block into an empty paragraph in its place, the caret in it, whatever follows it (the user's of 2026-11-09, twice: first the block went and the caret came to the start of the text after it with no paragraph made, then "Backspace makes the block an empty paragraph, always", as an empty heading and an empty container become one), where its box takes a paragraph (else it is deleted as a selected block is); the paragraph takes the alignment the block showed in the text area of its box (`align.mjs` `shownAlign()`: a picture, a card or a table set narrower and centred, or at the right, gives `doc-align-center` or `doc-align-right`; one at the left, or as wide as the area, none), which one more Backspace takes off ("Alignment and indent"), one more after that deleting the paragraph as Backspace in any empty paragraph does, the caret at the end of the text above. Enter makes an empty paragraph right after the block, the caret in it. A key typed makes a paragraph right after it holding the character (the browser types it into the field, whose `input` makes the paragraph), the caret at its end, and what is typed on goes into it; an input method composes in the field, where the caret is, and its words make the paragraph once it ends (`compositionend`). ↑ and ← put the caret at the end of the text before it in the page's order (the block's own last text: a table's last cell, a picture's caption; else the text before the block), ↓ and → at the start of the first after it, a formula block passed over; with none there, the caret stays. Esc selects the block. Ctrl+V makes the empty paragraph after it, which the paste then goes into. The text right below such a block comes back to this caret (the user's of 2026-11-09: Backspace in the empty paragraph under a table went into its last cell, under a callout into its box, and under a picture whose caption was out of sight past it, to the text before it): Backspace at the start of the text block right after the block in its box (`above()`: a paragraph, a heading, a list's first item, the list's block above it; never a table's cell or a code block's text), after that text block's own keys (a list item turns into a paragraph first) and its alignment and indent (`typing/` `afterAbove`, before the joins and removals of every block), puts the caret after the block: an empty text block goes as it does (one undo step, undo putting it back with the caret in it, redo this caret after the block again), one with words stays (no step), and a second Backspace is this caret's, as above; so the caret never goes into the block nor past it. ← and ↑ with the caret at the very start of that text block go there too (`up()`, before `caret.mjs` `arrow()`; ↑ from further along its first line still goes into a table above at that column), as ↓ and → from this caret come back to its start. A block above that takes no such caret and holds no text (a flowchart) is selected by Backspace, as before (`typing/`). Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Y undo and redo; other keys with Ctrl or ⌘, and Tab, do nothing. Each change is one undo step: Backspace's; Enter's; the paragraph a key made, with the words typed on in the same run (`history.mjs` `typedInto`: the first run of typing into the block a structural step made for it, begun within a second of it, is part of that step, undo taking it whole and redo putting the words back with it). Undo puts the caret after the block again (the step's `settle`), redo puts it in the paragraph. The caret goes when the focus leaves it on the page (a press elsewhere, a key that moves it), when its block leaves the page (`onSettle`), or when a change takes it; the window losing the focus keeps it, as the browser keeps its own caret. `window.__galleryAfter()` says the block it stands after (the browser tests).
- **Look.** What the editor's popups, bars, cards and notices are painted with is written once. `page/look.mjs` is a neutral palette (text, icon, background, hover, pressed, hairlines, field), two blues and two shadows as `:root` custom properties (`--ge-*`, `light-dark()`), in every document the editor draws in: the server puts the sheet in the head of the page it serves (it is there from the first paint, as the empty blocks' look is), `ui.mjs` `addStyleTo` adds it to any other document (the workbench's, for a picture viewed) and every sheet after it, once per text however often a menu or a crop window asks for its own; no style declares a token again (`tests/unit/editor-look.mjs` holds it). The blue is two tokens: `--ge-accent` (a switch, the chosen type, a table's tools and the bars that mark a place; never a button's fill) and `--ge-focus` (the focus ring); `--ge-fill`, `--ge-select` and `--ge-halo` are the tints (a box open on a formula, selected text, selected blocks). **The finish** (confirmed by the owner, 2026-11-17) is one rule per kind of thing that floats, all from tokens of the palette: a **card** (every menu, the format bar's panels, the link card, the formula editor and its groups' card, the icon picker and its popups, the media menu, a 批注's card, the outline's card, a board's settings, the conflict card and the lost texts' panel) is `--ge-r-card` 14px round with `--ge-shadow-card` (the 1px `--ge-outline`, then `0 2px 8px` 6% and `0 14px 40px` 16% black; in dark the line and `0 14px 40px` 50%), its rows `--ge-card-pad` 5px in, flush (no gap) and `--ge-r-row` 9px round; a **bar** on the text (the format bar and the table cells' bar, 6px round their tools; the bar of the Agent's changes, 44px high; the small bars on a picture) takes the lighter `--ge-shadow-bar` (`0 1px 2px` 6%, `0 6px 20px` 10%; dark `0 6px 20px` 36%); a **control** (a button, a tool, an icon button, a field, a cell of a grid) is `--ge-r-ctl` 8px round, a small one at a row's end or on a picture `--ge-r-small` 6px; the crop window and an embed in full screen, dialogs, `--ge-r-shell` 16px. Nothing is translucent and no line is thinner than 1px (they smear at 125%). The fills are the text's warm gray in three steps: `--ge-hover` 5% (a plain button or tool under the pointer), `--ge-hover-row` 7% (a row or a cell of a card under the pointer or the keys), `--ge-press` 10% (pressed); a row that takes something away for good is `--ge-danger-tint`, a pale red, behind its red words. Under the pointer a fill shows at once and fades out over 100ms ease-out when the pointer leaves, pressed it goes a step deeper at once, nothing scales (`BUTTONS`' `CONTROLS`, one rule for every control: no sheet of a control writes a transition of its own). Every glyph of one card or bar is of one weight: its icons 20px, its › and ✓ 16px, every line 1.25px (bold's B alone heavier, as what it stands for); the › and ✓ drawn as the workbench's menus and the kit's 下拉 and display menu draw theirs, and a group's title 8px under the row before it as theirs, so that a menu of the page and one of the app read as one hand (2026-11-17; the rows' icons stay 7px in, 1px more than the workbench's, where the block menu's grid has its icons). A switch is 30 × 18, its knob 14, `--ge-control-blue` on (`#4a81d4`, dark `#4f7fcb`) and `--ge-switch-track` off, 160ms, the knob 3px wider while pressed (the icon picker's 每次先选颜色, a card's settings). How a card comes and goes is `ui.mjs`: `appear()` grows it out of the point of it nearest what opened it (its transform-origin there), from .96 of its size and no opacity, 140ms `cubic-bezier(.23, 1, .32, 1)`, at once when the reader's last act was a key ("/" typed, Enter, →: `fromKeys()`, kept per window from keydown, pointerdown and a pointer that moved), its opacity alone for a reader who asks for reduced motion and for a card the reader types into (the link card, the formula editor, a menu with a search field, a list's 修改编号值, the icon picker, the media menu, a card's settings), so that the field never moves under the caret; `fadeAway()` takes a card off the page at once (what reads the page finds it gone, as before) and fades a picture of it out where it stood over 90ms: a copy in a closed shadow root of its own, inert and under no pointer, with copies of the page's sheets (each copied once), which no selector of the page reaches. The format bar's and the cells' bar's panels and the outline's card fade out the same 90ms. `--ge-shadow` stays a notice's; tooltips and notices keep their look. The icon picker carries `--c-*` variables of its own. The z-order of everything drawn over a document is one list (`src/shared/layers.mjs`: checks, layer, marquee, affordance, preview, mark, spin, guide, label, panel, toast, hint, menu, card, viewer, tooltip, each one above the one before; inside an overlay layer, `IN_LAYER`: tint, halo, frame, handle, drop line), and no style writes a number of its own. `!important` is where it has its reason: the one writer of the gesture locks (`gesture.mjs` `gestureLock`: the cursor a gesture asks for, and no selecting, wherever the pointer goes), a picture's inline cursor in its viewer, an open formula's tint over an empty block's bar and the layer's own position (the census is in `editor-look.mjs`). One tooltip (`tooltip.mjs`: the handle, the section's bar, the format bar, the picker's buttons, the bar of the Agent's changes, a custom page's note and the picture viewer's bar, over the workbench's window there, `how.host`; `showTooltipAt` for the picker, which waits as its own keys ask; warm along a row (a design review, 2026-11-17): one that went with the pointer leaving its control leaves the next `WARM` 300ms to show at once and with no fade, which a press, a scroll or a control left before its tooltip showed ends) and one bubble look for the hint that says why text stays read-only (`.ge-hint`). **A bubble stands only while its control is where the pointer found it** (the user's 2026-11-20: the pointer rested on a heading's level badge, its bubble came, the page scrolled and the bubble stayed where it was): `guardBubble(drop, node)` in `tooltip.mjs` is the one rule of the tooltip and of the hint, each watching its bubble while it shows and while it waits to show. It calls `drop` at once, with no fade, when anything scrolls (a `scroll` captured on the page's window, on the workbench's that holds it, `overlayHost().win`, so a scroller of the workbench's own counts, and on the window of the control), the wheel turns (also over what cannot scroll), the window loses the focus or the page is hidden or put away; and, once the bubble shows, at the first frame in which the control is taken out of the page, laid out as nothing (`display: none`), `visibility: hidden` or moved or resized by more than 2px (`DRIFT`, which a hover's slight scale does not reach): the browser tells none of that by `pointerleave` (a control moved from under the pointer is left when the pointer next moves, one taken out or hidden never is, and a heading's badge is dropped by the editor as the pointer goes off the heading). A wait that has not ended is ended too, so the bubble never comes late; what binds a bubble to its control is only the control as it was when the bubble showed (a card fading in under the pointer is no reason). A bubble over a rectangle (`tooltipAt`: a list's number, an outline guide) has no control to lose and goes with the scroll, the wheel, the blur. None of it shows again by itself: the pointer coming onto a control after that shows its own after the delay. A control needs no rule of its own for this (`hideTooltip` stays for the press and for a menu closing). Scenarios `tooltipsLeaveWithControls`, `hintsLeaveWithText`, `tooltipsInWorkbench`, and the kit's in `kitTooltip`. One bottom panel (`panel.mjs`): the conflicts' card (left) and the lost texts' (right) are the same popover, head with a red dot, quoted versions, the pale blue primary button. **One button look** (the user's rule, restated 2026-11-09: a button never carries a line or a ring, "我说过了很多次了"), `BUTTONS` in the palette's own sheet so that every document has it from the first paint and no style repeats it: `.ge-btn` (`ui.mjs` `textButton`) is a text button, 28px high, no line and no fill, `--ge-hover` under the pointer and while the keys are on it, `--ge-press` pressed; `data-primary` is the pale blue fill, always shown, its words the deeper blue (`--ge-btn-primary*`, the workbench's `--btnPriBg`, a tint, never `--ge-accent` with white words), `data-danger` with it the pale red (a delete), `data-quiet` a small plain one for looking, not deciding (收起, 定位, 关闭); a button made of a div says it cannot be pressed by `.disabled` or `aria-disabled` (dimmed to `--ge-off`, an opacity of .4, as a menu's row that cannot be taken and a category of the icon picker that has nothing). A button's words are of the text's weight, a primary's 500, 8px either side (until 2026-11-17 every one 500, 10px). `.ge-icon-btn` is an icon button standing alone (a 批注's marker and its delete): the icon goes darker under the pointer and the gray comes only while it is pressed. It is what the conflict card, the lost texts' panel, the bar of the Agent's changes (撤回, 确认, 撤销), the crop window (保存, 取消), a list's 确定, the icon picker's 保存, 取消 and 返回 and the media menu's 上传文件, 选择文件 and 嵌入链接 (the primaries of their tabs) are made of; the round + of a flowchart, of a gap between columns and the table's corner handle, which float over the page, have no line either, only a soft shadow to lift them. What keeps a thin outline is a field (the link's address, a search, a formula's TeX, the inputs of a card's 设置), which is not a button (the icon picker's drop zone has its field's fill and no line, and a card's 打开 pick, a button drawn as a field, a gray fill, deeper under the pointer and while its menu is open, and no line); the tools of a bar (the format bar, the link card, the image's bar) and a menu's rows are gray under the pointer, and deeper pressed (`--ge-press`, a formula's groups and symbols, the crop's shapes too). The keys on any of them (`:focus-visible`) give it the same gray and no line (`BUTTONS`' `FOCUSED` list, an icon button's too: a 批注's marker had a 2px blue outline until 2026-11-17, a dozen tools the browser's ring); a color's swatch and a switch, which a fill cannot show on, take a ring (`RING`: 2px of the surface, then 2px of `--ge-focus`). A chosen shape of the crop is the gray of a chosen tab, no blue. The palette's sheet gives what is selected in what the editor draws (a field, a card's words) Gallery's blue (`::selection` at `--ge-select`; a block's, a chip's and the formula field's own rules say more), and a notice the text's own ink (`--ge-toast-bg`, #2c2c2b in light; #0f0f0f, darker than any text, until 2026-11-17). `editor-look.mjs` holds it: the rules of `BUTTONS` draw no line, a primary is the pale token, and no editor style of a button writes a line, a ring or the solid blue itself. In the kit the same rule stands for what it draws at run time: the consent card's 允许 (`base.css`) is a pale blue primary, the reader's display button an icon button, and a detail card's entry in the text has its fill and no ring.
- **Overlays.** What the editor draws over the page (a selected block's fill, the dashed frame of the text being edited, the red frame of a flagged block (`flags.mjs`: a conflict, a new block with no place in the file; 2px past the block, as the fill is, never an outline of the block's own; the editor's red, `--ge-diff-del-text`, of the words taken out and the card's dot, not the kit's chart color `--doc-danger` it was until 2026-11-17), the handle and a section's bar, a drag's line, tint and copy, a box selection, a resize's lines) is never part of the report: each node carries `data-doc-dev`, and undo never records one. A struct step records only the document's nodes of the containers it touches (`history.mjs`), and undo and redo leave the editor's nodes where they are; the body is one of those containers when a block stands right in the page, and recording the editor's nodes there once made undo put back a converted block's old fill, which then stayed on the screen with nothing to update or remove it. Each overlay belongs to something (`overlays.mjs` `own`: a selected block, the block the handle stands beside, the text framed, a gesture under way; it marks the node `data-ge-overlay`, and the audit and the snapshot go through the marked nodes, so whatever is owned is audited: the fills, frame, handle, drag, box and resize drawings, the column gaps' line and pin, the image and embed tools, the tab +, the table tools and their drag ghost, the link card's marks, the Agent's changes' fills, the words taken out shown in a text block and the deleted blocks' ghosts (`changes/`)) and goes the moment that ends, never on a later frame: the fills follow the selection as it changes (`selection/` `render` through `fills.mjs`, the one painter of fills over blocks (blue for the blocks selected, warm for a 批注's, green for what an Agent added or changed): one per selected block on the page, kept by block), and after every structural change, undo and redo each part drops at once what stands for a block no longer on the page (`settle`). Drawings over blocks go into the layer of the coordinate system the block moves in (`overlay-layer.mjs`): the page's (absolute at the document's origin, so scrolling the page carries them natively), the window's (for what is fixed or sticky, as a table's header cell) or the one inside the open modal dialog holding the block. Positions are measured against the layer's own rectangle, one measurement per block per frame (`shapeOf`), so a block's fill, frame and handle agree; the room it has above and below it (`roomOf`, which measures its neighbours) is measured only when read, by a selected block's fill or the frame of what is edited, so the handle, the flags and the other drawings never measure the neighbours. One animation-frame loop (`frames.mjs`) keeps them on their blocks through reflow and boxes that scroll by themselves: tasks woken by what changes, one frame requested at a time however many ask, from inside a frame too. It rests while nothing moves: a task that draws over blocks (the handle and a section's bar, the fills, the frame of what is edited, the flags) says so (`FOLLOW`) and runs again only when something may have moved them (a mutation of the page outside the editor's layers, anything scrolled, the window or the page resized, a transition or an animation under way until it ends, a picture, a frame or fonts loaded, the pointer onto another element, the focus moving, a task woken), and on three frames after each; only a gesture's task (a drag, a box selection or a text selection scrolling by the window's edge) runs on every frame. Run on every frame while anything showed, it cost 60–160ms of the main thread a second with the pointer resting on a paragraph or blocks selected, for a picture that did not change. A task that throws takes down what it draws (a drag is cancelled, never dropped; the selection stays) and the error is said, at most every five seconds. A gesture ends idempotently however it ends (letting go, Esc, the pointer capture lost, the window losing the focus, the page hidden: `gesture.mjs`, written once for resizing, a gap between columns, a box selection and a selection across blocks; a gap dragged whose row the page's own script takes away is over at the next move), and a press drags once: a drag it started and that was cancelled does not start again. After each frame (and after a scroll, a press or a key) the overlays are audited: one standing for nothing is a bug, said on the console with a snapshot, and removed. The audit goes through the marked nodes it found and those owned since, and looks through the whole page again only once a node it does not hold may have come onto it (an observer of what is added, from one audit to the first such node: a copy of an overlay, one taken off and put back). `window.__gallerySelected()` is the selection's blocks (the page carries no mark of them), and `window.__galleryOverlays()` describes everything drawn (its owner, where it is against where it should be), the frames, the layers, the selection and the last events; `copy(__galleryOverlays())` in DevTools puts it on the clipboard, to document something drawn wrong. The rule for anything drawn: it never waits, to go, for a callback that may not come (the next frame, an animation's end, a pointerup). A heading's fold triangle is one too (`heading fold`, owned by its heading, with `expected`: the place `foldRectOf` gives, so a triangle that did not follow its heading's last line is told), and so is its level badge beside it (`heading level`, expected at `levelRectOf`).
- **Copying blocks.** `clipboard/`: `index.mjs` handles the events, `own/` is the clipboard's own format (`describe.mjs` what selected blocks are, `blocks.mjs` checking and making them again, `export.mjs` what other programs get, `writers.mjs` the writers by kind, which the page export shares), `import/` is another program's content (see "Pasting"). `own/` reads the block's definition (`clipboard`: "The clipboard field" below) and names only the default paragraph, the inline marks and the code host; `index.mjs` keeps where blocks go, and `import/` maps outside formats to block types in one place. With blocks selected (and no text), Ctrl+C copies them and Ctrl+X cuts them (copied, then taken out as Delete would, one undo step; what may not go stays: a card whose copy is of its unit on the page alone (a chart card), alone or in a container, which a cut takes off it, so a cut that holds one is refused with a notice and takes nothing off the page, as the paste of such a copy elsewhere is refused (`unmadeCopy(null, blocks)`), and so is a cut whose description the paste would not take (`checkBlocks` refuses the clipboard's data whole, and the paste then takes its HTML: a link where a file block was), its notice the same (`cutRefusal` in `index.mjs`, a selection across blocks cut too), each block checked whatever their number (`checkBlocks(…, { limit: Infinity })`); more than a paste takes at once (500) are pasted as their HTML, text as text as a cut always was, so such a cut goes, but one holding a block copied as itself (a media block, a table, a row of columns, which would come back as a link or a picture of an address) is refused; a table, a row of columns and the four media blocks, whose copies carry their data, are cut and pasted as any block): the clipboard gets a description the editor pastes as blocks again (`application/x-gallery-blocks`, written beside the same data under the type it had before 2026-10-26, `application/x-report-studio-blocks`, so that a page of the old version open in another tab pastes it, and read from it when the new one is not there, `ownData`: each block's type, inline HTML and color (a paragraph's or a heading's alignment and indent with its colors: "Alignment and indent"), and what lists, quotes, panels, disclosures and sections hold, a list item's own lists with it; an item selected on its own carries its list's kind, `list`), and for other programs plain HTML (`p`, `h1`–`h4`, lists with "[x] " before a checked to-do, `blockquote`, `details`, `pre > code.language-x`, `table`, formulas as `$…$` and `$$…$$`, colors as the kit's classes with their color in a style (a background on words its words' fill)) and Markdown as `text/plain` (`#` headings, `- ` / `1. ` / `- [ ] ` items, an item's lists 4 spaces in, `> ` quotes, `<aside>` for a highlight block, fenced code, `$$` formula blocks (an empty one is left out: no `$$ $$` to read back as nothing), `|` tables (a merged cell's other slots empty; the HTML keeps `colspan` and `rowspan`), links as their text; a list's items one line apart, other blocks an empty line), both of which read back as the same blocks. Items copied on their own are written as a list of their kind for other programs (`groupItems` in `own/rules.mjs`: each run of them one list, as the paste makes them where no list takes them). Blocks the page can make are copied as what they are: text of every type (the opening summary as a highlight panel), lists, quotes, panels, disclosures, code (opened for editing or not), formula blocks (their TeX; an empty one copies as an empty one), dividers, sections holding only those and tabs areas (each tab's name, emoji and blocks). A card (a chart card, an image, a file, a bookmark or an embed), a table or a row of columns is copied as itself (`type: "copy"`, naming the document and the block's unit): its data is in its markup (every chart declared, linkage by bound keys), so a paste in the same page shows a copy of the one on the page (what the kit drew taken off with `DocKit.dehydrate`, its block id, ids and the editor's marks too, its fields still editable; the kit's runtime draws it again), and the commit sends `{ copy: unit }`, for which the server writes a copy of that unit's source as the file has it then, its block ids and ids taken out, as it puts back a deleted unit (`copiedSource()` in `source/text.mjs`); but for the ids the copy names itself (an SVG's marker or gradient by `url(#id)`, a symbol by `href="#id"` or `xlink:href`, a field by a label's `for`, anything by an attribute that holds ids: `idsNamed` in `src/shared/copy-ids.mjs`, what `keepReferenced` reads too), which keep a prefix of the copy's own (`c1-`, the first no id of the file, or of the copies of the same commit, starts with) with every reference in it written to match, so its arrows draw from its own markers, its label is its own field's and nothing of it holds the original from being deleted; the page does the same to the copies it shows (`copyOf(…, { editable })` keeps them, `own/ids.mjs prefixCopies()` after `relinkCopies()` prefixes them from the page's ids: the two prefixes may differ, each unique where it stands); the page takes it in as any unit it made. A table also carries its data (`data`: the table as the page describes it to the server, `tableData()` in `components/table/index.mjs`: its cells' inline HTML with a cell's lists, its header row and column, its cells' looks (colors, alignment, bold), the columns' widths, a merged cell's spans, 识别数字列), so pasted in another document, or once the block is gone, it is made anew from it, without the frame's size or the scope line (those go only with the copy of its unit within the document) (`newTable()` builds from that description, `validTable()` checks it, for the clipboard as for the server's `file.mjs`; a table an Agent wrote by hand may say what that description does not (a cell with a text color and a fill, a merged cell across a header of several rows, a width that is not px), so `carriableTable()` makes of it what `validTable()` accepts: one color to a cell, a width only where it is one, the merged cells taken apart where the header would be crossed, so that the copy, which is checked as a whole, is never refused for its data); a chart card there is refused with the reason, which names what it is. The four media blocks (an image, a file, an embed, a bookmark: the user's of 2026-11-20, "图片竟然是不能剪切的") carry their data the same way (`clipboard.data`: what a new one is written with, its definition's `extras`, an image's alt words too: an image `{ src, caption, classes, span, original, crop, alt }`, a file `{ href, name, size, caption }`, an embed `{ src, height, span, caption }`, a bookmark `{ href, title, description, icon, cover, caption }`; a web page's address read off the card or frame as the file has it, where a page served marks only files of the folder; none for one that shows what that cannot say, an Agent's that the page never writes, of which the data would say an empty block or leave a part out: a picture, a file's link, a bookmark's icon or cover of a web or `data:` address, a frame of a page of no https: address, a frame or a card of a class of its own; and none where what it says is no data the paste takes, `dataOf` in `own/describe.mjs` checking it as the paste does (`checkBlocks`: a size the page never writes, `2.3 MB`, a name with `:` or `?`, an image's words of two lines or past 1000, a width written `0.50`), so that such a block is copied as before the media blocks carried data: within its document a copy of its source, its cut refused, not pasted in another document (it was pasted as a link with the port in it, its cut lost), checked as the file's writers take it (`clipboard.check`: a file of the folder, a side of the image's, a width a share of the reading width, words of one line; the caption inline HTML made again by `pastedLines`), and made anew of it where their unit is not (cut, another document: `clipboard.build`, the element `place` puts in, of the same parts, `pictureParts`, `fileParts`, `frameParts`, `cardParts` in their `index.mjs`), so that one cut pastes back as it was, its own file; within its document a copy whose unit is still there is a copy of its source, as before. A copy carrying data is its unit only while the unit, described again, carries the same data (`copied()`; one carrying none, a chart card or a picture of a web address, and an instance carrying its markup, while the unit's markup is what was copied, `html`: two pictures of a web address with no caption say the same words; text blocks are copied as their HTML and never as a unit): once the page numbers its units anew (opened again, or switched to and back, after a cut) its number names another block, and two images with no caption say the same words, none, so that the file would copy the other one's source (b, b where a, b were) and no undo would bring the cut one back. Into another document the files of the folder they name (`clipboard.files`: an image's picture and the one it was cropped from, a file's, an embed's file, a bookmark's icon and cover; `filesOf` in `own/blocks.mjs`, in boxes and copies' data too) are copied first (`clipboard/carry.mjs`): each read where the server hands out that document's files (the `file` route, its bytes as they are, in that document's workspace) and sent to this one's folder as an upload of its kind, which keeps it by its content's hash (`media-store.mjs`): the same bytes, never another document's file of the same name overwritten, the same file kept once, one Gallery put there keeping its name (it is named so already), one an Agent named taking the folder's (`image-<hash>.png`); then the blocks are made naming the copies (`what.fileHere(path)`, shown at `what.served(route, path)`), one undo step with the paste; a file the upload refuses (too large, no picture) stops the paste with the reason, nothing half made. A row of columns carries its columns the same way (each its share and its blocks described, a picture in one with its data), so it is cut and pasted into another document too; a chart card in a column stops that as a chart card alone does. The clipboard names the document with its workspace (`project`: the page's /~ws-… base, where it is not the first; two workspaces may each hold a document of the same name), which `copied()` and the paste's `same` go by (`sameDocument`). For other programs a media block's data is written thus (`clipboard.said`): an image `![words](address)` and `<p><img src alt></p>`, a file, an embed and a bookmark a link of their caption, name or title, the caption a paragraph after it, a file of the folder by the whole address this page's server serves it at (`how.served`: Word fetches the picture while Gallery runs). An image copied alone (Ctrl+C or Ctrl+X with it the one block selected, or 复制图片 in its block menu) goes on the system clipboard as a picture too (`clipboard/picture.mjs`; its definition's `picture`): the copy event's `clipboardData` takes strings alone, so the event writes what it always wrote, and then `navigator.clipboard.write` replaces that with a ClipboardItem of a PNG of the picture (drawn on a canvas as large as its file is: a JPEG, a WebP, a GIF's first frame; an SVG at least as large as it shows) and HTML of an `<img>` by its whole address, as the browser's own 复制图片 writes (a bitmap and that HTML, which the user found pastes as a picture elsewhere), whose attribute `data-gallery-blocks` holds the clipboard's own data (`HTML_MARK`; a paste event's clipboardData has no other place for it, a type of the page's own being the async API's alone), which `ownData` reads where the own type is not there: pasted in Gallery it is the image as it was, its own file byte for byte, never that PNG (the paste takes no picture files beside the page's own data). The PNG is made while the write waits on it (a promise in the item, asked for in the gesture); a copy or cut of the page meanwhile wins (the PNG refused, the clipboard as that copy left it), and so does one made anywhere else (the workbench around the page, another document's frame, another program), whose copy event the page never sees but which takes the focus from it first: a write asked while the page had the focus goes only while it still has it (`document.hasFocus()`); where the browser refuses the write (no permission, no ClipboardItem, a picture too large for a canvas) the copy event's data stays. Blocks copied with an image: HTML and Markdown, no PNG. The browser's own 复制图片 (its context menu) is as it was. Scenarios `mediaCutPaste` and `imageToClipboard`. A card, a table or a row of columns inside a container (a panel, a disclosure, a tab) goes with it: its copy is of its unit, and the container made on the page holds it (the commit's `{ copy }`). A chart drawn from a table names it by a key (`data-doc-chart-data` on its host, `data-doc-chart-source` on the table's frame: docs/render.md, "Charts from a table"), and so does a view of a table (`data-doc-from` on its instance: "Table views"), the table's readers (`TABLE_READERS`), which what a paste makes of either carries, a copy of it, a table made again of its data (`tableData`'s `chart`: a table cut, one whose original is gone or changed since the copy, or one of another document) or what a clipboard carried of an instance: pasted together, a reader and its table are a pair of their own, both taking the first key of `t1`, `t2` … the page has not, so two pairs never cross (a board copied with its table is a board of the copy), in the document they were copied from or another; a table pasted without its reader keeps its key while no table on the page carries it (a cut table put back, one whose original was deleted: its card draws it again), and is a plain table otherwise and in another document (its key taken off: a reader reads the first table of its key, which could be the copy, and another document's are no readers of it); a reader pasted alone keeps its key and reads the same table as its original, in its document; in another (the clipboard's `report` not this one's: `same`, `index.mjs` `origin`) it takes it off, as its table is not there and one there carrying the key (keys are `t1`, `t2` … in every document) is no table of its: a view of no table, never one reading and changing that one (`relinkCopies` in `own/chart-keys.mjs`, which counts what templates hold too; a view's new key is the instance's attribute, which its commit sends as `from`, and one carried as markup with its key taken off sends `from` null, written with none). A copy's new keys ride on it (`COPY_KEYS`, `data-ge-chart-keys`) and go with it as `{ copy, keys: { old: new | null } }` (checked by `copyKeys`, `components/figure/`), which `copiedSource()` writes into the copy's source; a table made again is written with its key on its frame (`components/table/file.mjs`, checked by `validTable`). The page chose those keys from what it shows, and the file may hold more by the time the commit lands (an Agent's chart table added meanwhile, which the intake gave the first key not taken, maybe the same one; another tab's paste): so `commitEdits` makes sure of the keys in what it made (copies, tables made again, units put back, a view made from a menu) and on the tables whose key it set (the attribute a view made from a menu puts on a table with none: `keyed`) against the file as it leaves it (`source/chart-keys.mjs` `relinkMade`, the same rules with the whole file in view: a pair that meets the key elsewhere takes a new one, a table alone whose key another table carries loses it; it repairs, never refuses, as the reader could not see the key a conflict would be about) and answers what it changed (`chartKeys: { old: new | null }`), which the page does to the blocks the commit made and the tables it keyed, and to the undo steps that recorded them (`apply` in `page/commit.mjs`, which returns them as `rekeyed` for the editor's `committed` to give `history.mjs` `rekeyed`; the kit's watcher of the keyed tables tells each chart and view, which read their table again). A document's own widget is bound to the document's script or its ids, and the copy stops with the reason (the Agent copies those); so does a block not yet saved. Pasted, they are new blocks, built by what each definition says (`clipboard.build`, or the `build` of its `page.mjs`, the same builders the menus make them with) and written like any made on the page (see "Pasting"). The clipboard is not trusted: `checkBlocks()`, for the clipboard's own data only (another program's content is made to fit, see "Pasting"), checks the description (known types, a list holding items, a quote paragraphs, no section in a section, what each container takes, at most 500 blocks) and every piece of inline HTML goes through `pastedLines()` again, so only the format bar's marks (and formulas) are left. A folded heading selected copies its section with it (`fold/` `takenWith`); what is pasted is not folded (a copy has no identity, and a fold is the heading's).
- **Copying text.** `clipboard/own/selection.mjs`. Text selected in one text block (the browser's selection: a list item's words by a double click or a drag, a heading's, a cell's, a part of code…) copies as that text and nothing more: the clipboard's own data is its inline HTML (`{ v: 1, report, html }`: `selectedHtml`, as `serializeInner` writes a block's: the format bar's marks, those around it too, so a word selected in bold is bold; a chip as its source, a cell's lists; code as its text with `<br>` between its lines), the range kept to the block's own text (an item's ends before the blocks it holds) and widened over a chip an end stands inside, an end at a chip's very start or a start at its very end leaving it out, as the selection shows (`ownRange`: Chrome's triple click stops at a formula's start, the formula not drawn); its spaces are those the page shows (`shownSpaces`): a space selected before or after the words goes with them, each run of white space is one space, and an end of it that is an end of the block's text has none, the line breaks and indentation of an Agent's source around a block's words (a line break and spaces between `<p>` and its words) that the page does not show (a non-breaking space typed there stays; code keeps its spaces as they are); a selection ending at the start of a block an item holds (a triple click in such an item) is the item's text, one holding some of that block the browser's (`endsAtHeld`); other programs get that HTML (`textFormats`: formulas as `$…$`, colors in a style, a link shown with its icon a link) and its words as plain text (`plainText`: a formula its TeX, a line break or a cell's item a line of its own). No block goes with it: no list's marker, no heading's size, no to-do's box (the browser's own copy wrapped an item's words in its list, which the import read as a list of one line, "1. " before them; a part of code or a cell's words came back as a code block or a table). Pasted at a caret it goes in as text with its marks and its line breaks (`pasteRich` with `breaks`: the text keeps its "\n"), in code and in a field of plain text as its plain text, in a cell with the marks the cell takes, with blocks selected as a paragraph after them. Ctrl+X copies it and deletes it as typing would (`execCommand("delete")`: one undo step). A selection across blocks (`text-select/`, `copyAcross`) copies as blocks, each of its type (`describeAcross`), the first from where the selection starts, those between whole (as a copy of blocks describes them; a block the page cannot make, a document's own widget, as its words, which a cut refuses: nothing is cut, with the notice a copy of blocks gives), the last up to where it ends; a list's items each an item of its kind (`list`, as an item selected on its own) with its checked state and what of the blocks it holds the selection reaches; a quote's paragraphs in a quote; a highlight block, a disclosure or a section the selection ends in, with what of it comes before the end (its icon, its title); a flowchart it holds some or all of a flowchart, with its direction (its steps are nodes, no items: never their blocks as loose paragraphs), each node it holds whole a node with its blocks, icon, color, direction and weight, the node it ends in a node with what of it comes before the end, the one it starts in its blocks alone (as a highlight block it starts in), its first number where it holds the first node; a tabs area it ends in with the tab shown and what of its panel comes before the end (a tabs area holds the content it shows; its tab row is no text of it, and none of it is copied when the end is before its panel); in a detail card it goes through the card and its panel (neither blocks nor boxes); a title is a paragraph (no block of its own); were none of it found among the blocks, its texts would be paragraphs (a copy is never empty). Around where it starts, a highlight block, a disclosure, a section, an item whose own text comes before the start, a row of columns, a column or a tabs area (its tabs not shown left out) are not copied, only what of them it holds. Other programs get them as any copied blocks. Pasted, they are blocks as any copied (see "Pasting"): at a caret in a block with text, a paragraph first joins the text before the caret and any other first block follows it (an item first stays an item), the text after the caret goes on in the last; in an empty paragraph they take its place; items in a list of their kind are its items, the list at the caret or one right before or after them (scenarios `copyTextInBlock`, `copyAcross`).
- **Pasting.** `clipboard/index.mjs`.
